## §30 — THE COARSE SECTOR IS OPEN, NOT PASSED: AD'S ROW FAILS AT THE ALIGNED ENVIRONMENT WHILE PM'S WALL PASSES ON AVERAGE, AND THE ONE OBJECT BOTH POINT AT IS AN ENVIRONMENT-AVERAGED CRITERION — THEOREM U STATED WITH ITS PROOF PLAN; THE FIBRE'S CONVEX DOMAIN CERTIFIED TO 1/16 AND BRACKETED BY 0.40, SO THE SMALL-FIELD ROUTE TO (ii) IS CLOSED AT 2.2; THE HOUSE INSTRUMENT IN GAWK REPRODUCES THE ANCHOR TO THIRTEEN DIGITS (2026-11-02 15:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat iii-43; every report verbatim in `records/agents/2026-11-02/read/REPORTS.md`)

**30.1 THE TWO BAND READINGS PULL OPPOSITE WAYS, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.**
- **AD (the averaged deficit at a_c = 4a; `math/AD/WORK.md`): THE KILL-SWITCH FIRES.** On the blocked SU(2) measure at β = 2.2 with n = 4 (24⁴, the exterior integrated by heat bath, a score-matching fit of the blocked action — a READING), the single-link averaged L² row at the ORDERED environment is 1.1639 (R_ord v = 1.1639 v with v = 1, the quadrature to 4·10⁻¹³), so the deficit 1 − Σ R e^{κ|·|} ≤ −0.164 ± 0.025 at every κ ≥ 0 (6.6σ; 32⁴ gives −0.154); the six-link loops carry it (a link sits in 72 chairs and 24 parallelograms at θ ≈ 0.0056 each, adding ≈ 0.55 to the plaquette's 0.578). IT IS A SUPREMUM FAILURE: at three Monte Carlo environments the row reads 0.816, 0.776, 0.776, and at a Haar environment 0.801 — typical environments sit below one; M2's Theorem 3.1 needs the ess sup. Only the single-link form was computed (THE GROUND's coarse-point form, SU(2)⁴ per site, estimated at 0.64 from plaquettes), so the finding is "the single-link averaged row fails at 4a on the fitted action", not "Hypothesis I fails"; the single-link form refuses even fine Wilson at 0.2452, where Theorem 8.7 certifies, and refuses every β > 0.2226809 by a PROVED bound (the row ≥ 18·I₂/I₁). Teeth through the same chain: Villain U(1) at β_V = 2, 4, 8 refused at every block size (rows 13–18, the coarse U(1) coupling RISING with n); SU(2) at β = 0.2452, n = 4, CERTIFIED 0.030 by a one-term Birkhoff bound; the massive-Gaussian known answers met on two roads to 10⁻¹⁴. PROVED on its page: Lemma A (the averaging map built on one axial tree per block, the same for every direction, has no perimeter self-energy — the trunk's `avg_link` in `photon_gauss.py` used a different tree per direction, which is why its "averaged" column grew 0.489 → 1.449: THAT COLUMN OF 28.1 IS RELABELLED a different map; tooth 1's pass stands, its ratio being read from long-distance correlations and map-independent); Lemma B (ρ ≤ tanh(Δ/4), Birkhoff–Hopf); Lemma C (any explicit environment gives a lower bound on the ess-sup row — the form of every refusal certificate); Lemma D (a von Mises–Fisher tilt cannot raise a one-loop pair's correlation at first order; the free environment is the maximum, 196-point scan); LEMMA E, the multiplicity law (the row is (1/4)·Σ_{ℓ∋c}(|ℓ|−1)|t_ℓ|; polygons through one link number 6, 114, 2868 at lengths 4, 6, 8 — a coarse action passes only if its couplings fall faster than this polygon entropy). At a_c = 8a the couplings are unresolved at 150 configurations and the crossover of this criterion from 2.2 lies in (4a, 8a] as a resolution-limited reading.
- **PM (MIR's pair moments at the wall; `math/PM/WORK.md`): PASS, AS A READING.** The two-step wall floor, with 22 of the 28 loop links integrated exactly given the rest and the exterior averaged over its law (Lemma D1, the tower property, PROVED: this is the number the ring-integrated object converges to), reads [0, 0.0030] for the planar pair and [0, 0.0044] bent, against the certificate's ceiling 0.016175 — E[χ₁(A)χ_½(B)]_c = −3.3·10⁻⁹ ± 3.5·10⁻⁸ on 16⁴, 384 configurations, 15–35× tighter than the 10⁻⁶ asked. Two limits belong with it: it covers one bond type of the KP condition in the certificate's character-priced form only (MIR's crude L¹ form fails at two steps by ≈ ×300 on the one-plaquette term, a desk estimate), and IT IS NOT A GAP TEST — the free photon at SU(2)'s own coarse disorder passes the same wall (0.0019). The U(1) arm: at two steps the test refuses compact U(1) at 1.2 (×39) and Villain at β_V = 2 (×190; monopoles below 10⁻⁶ there), separating SU(2) from U(1) THROUGH DISORDER, and Villain passes at five steps (10.6 → 3.15 → 0.80 → 0.077 → 0.0006) — MIR's Lemma 7.2 seen on the floor. ONE STEP FAILS (planar floor [0.196, 0.222]): the wall is crossed between the first and second exact step. The nearest axis is the spin-½ one (c_½ ≤ 0.060 against the ceiling 0.12809, margin ×2.1). ⟨½ tr W(4×4)⟩ = 0.0013006 ± 0.0000062 replaces the page's 0.00136. What would make it PROVED: a certified bound on the sampled exterior's influence (no convergent expansion exists at 2.2), or certified bounds on E[χ₁χ₁] and E[χ_½χ_½] with reflection positivity in the shared-edge plane bounding the cross moment by their geometric mean — nothing in the program provides either.
- **Read together:** a supremum criterion fails at the aligned environment, which is Haar-rare and is not a phase; an averaged criterion passes, and passes the photon too. The coarse sector's hypothesis (i) is OPEN in every form the record can read, and the object both readings point at is a criterion that averages over environments instead of taking the worst — stated next.

**30.2 THEOREM U — UNIQUENESS AND CLUSTERING FROM A GOOD-ENVIRONMENT ROW AND A PRICED BAD SET (the desk; the full statement, proof plan, inputs and falsifier on `reduction/theorem_u.md` in the records).** For a translation-invariant reflection-positive specification (the blocked measure's, MIR Lemma 2.1), call the environment ω GOOD at y if the Dobrushin row AT ω, ρ_y(ω), is ≤ 1 − η, BAD otherwise (a local event). THEOREM U (conditional): if (U1) the row is ≤ 1 − η on good environments, (U2) the bad event is priced uniformly over the RP Gibbs class — μ(B_{y₁} ∩ … ∩ B_{y_n}) ≤ εⁿ for well-separated sites, by the chessboard estimate on the periodic blocked measure — and (U3) ε·(N+1)·e^a + (1 − η)·e^a < 1 (the Kotecký–Preiss form of "disagreement does not percolate"), then the Gibbs measure is unique in the RP class and clusters exponentially at rate a. Proof plan from classical pieces: van den Berg–Maes' disagreement coupling with the environment-dependence of the per-site bound kept rather than sup'd (U-A); the bad set sparse under the coupling by (U2), the argument run as one-site perturbations on the torus so that every measure in play is periodic and RP (U-B); the mixed-percolation path sum with the bad sites as marked polymers (U-C). Every compact G. THE INPUTS TODAY: (U1) q ≈ 0.78–0.82 at 4a in the single-link form (AD's typical rows, READINGS), q ≈ 0.7 expected in the coarse-point form; (U2) the bad set is the near-aligned environment, Haar-rare (MIR: the aligned probability of a coarse plaquette is the Haar value, 0.058 at 30°), priced by the chessboard estimate on a near-Haar measure (MIR's potential 10⁻³, PM's sup|R − 1| ≤ 1.9·10⁻⁵) — STAKED ε ≤ 10⁻³ at 45° with k ≥ 4 links; (U3) with q = 0.82 and N ≈ 100 (the chair and parallelogram couplings reach range 2) needs ε < 8·10⁻⁴ — BORDERLINE in the single-link form, which is why the coarse-point form is where the theorem should be read. THE REFUTERS INSIDE IT: Villain U(1) has NO good set (rows 13–18 at every environment, AD's T1), so Theorem U refuses it by (U1), not by averaging; the free photon at SU(2)'s disorder has the massless Gaussian's row ≥ 1 at every environment (M2c's Lemma P): refused. The theorem cannot pass a gapless theory through its averaged form, because a gapless Gaussian's row is above one everywhere, not only at rare environments — and it is silent, correctly, where an aligned phase would exist ((U2) fails for that state). What it does and does not do: it gives (i) a hypothesis the exact engines CAN read (rows at sampled environments plus a chessboard-priced tail) in place of a supremum they cannot; it does not remove (ii) — (U2)'s uniform price needs the blocked potential's size, which is the fibre's clustering again, and the rows are read on a fitted action whose certification is (ii).

**30.3 THE FIBRE'S CONVEX DOMAIN — CVX's CERTIFICATE CLOSES THE SMALL-FIELD ROUTE TO (ii) AT 2.2 (the proof agent CVX; `math/CVX/WORK.md` §§0–26).** THEOREM CVX (COMPUTED in verified IEEE arithmetic on PROVED lemmas; M8's additive exponential coordinates, the linear slice V): on the (4,2,2,2) torus with half-open L = 2 blocks every background with all link angles ≤ 1/16 has λ_min(Hess S|_V) ≥ 0.0192; at L = 3 (centred blocks, link angles ≤ 2294/65536) λ_min ≥ 0.0186. The proof: H = H₀ + H₁(B) + R(B) with H₁ the cubic vertex (exactly the Hessian of the four-determinant V₃, by an exact rational Taylor expansion), Lemma M (colour covariance turns the cubic vertex into one 4×4 Hermitian majorant inequality per plaquette position), Lemma R (r ⪰ −16b²h₀ − (9.989b² + 40.27b³ + 42.67b⁴)I from exact integer Taylor tensors and a Dyson bound), one verified Cholesky per b — no cover of the 270-dimensional background family, which cannot be done. AGAINST M8b: a certified non-convex direction at b′ = 0.40133 (a three-point second difference −2.23·10⁻⁵ in 60-digit interval arithmetic), so THE CERTIFIED BRACKET IS 1/16 ≤ b_cvx ≤ 0.40133 at L = 2 and M8b's "Wilson convex to 0.50" IS REFUTED (its heat-kernel, Manton and DHZ brackets are untested and suspect); and the quadratic-majorant method itself caps near b ≈ 0.08–0.1 (Lemma B: every quadratic majorant is colour-blind). THE NIELSEN–OLESEN THRESHOLD with the covariant block constraint is CERTIFIED in (0.5890, 0.6872) at L = 2 ((0.2327, 0.3103) at L = 3); without the constraint every quantised field is unstable; a weak field first RAISES the constrained minimum (1.850 → 2.125). FLOORS: M8's accounting at the certified radii gives β ≥ 378 (L = 2), and no convexity-based floor on this slice can go below 9.17; M8b's 5.9 rested on the refuted reading. WHAT THIS DOES TO §29.2: the fibre's small parameter lives on the convex domain; that domain is certified to link radius 1/16 and bracketed by 0.40, while the typical fine link at 2.2 has θ_rms ≈ 0.35. So the domain on which the fibre's Gaussian reference is massive by certificate is far smaller than the typical fluctuation — the large-field region of the fibre at 2.2 is not a tail to be priced but the bulk of the measure, and its physics is the Nielsen–Olesen instability: charged modes condense in the background's own field strength. THEOREM F OF §29.2 CANNOT BE PROVED BY THE SMALL-FIELD ROUTE AT β = 2.2; the sentence "the fibre is a massive field expanded about a Gaussian with a small parameter" is WITHDRAWN, dated, as a statement about the measure (it is true on the convex domain, which the measure does not fill). What survives: (F1) the Gaussian reference's mass at the ordered background (PROVED, two roads); the per-plaquette smallness of the quartic term AT a convex background; the three lemmas' structure. The band's open core, with today's coordinates: (a) an environment-averaged criterion for the coarse sector (Theorem U, its two numbers to read); (b) the fibre's clustering at 2.2 OUTSIDE the convex domain — the charged sector at O(1) field strength — for which no route in the record gives a proof: the readings pipeline, the sup criteria, the KP route and the convexity route each refuse at 2.2 by their own kill-switches, and the record now says so for each.

**31.2 Theorem U′ — the environment-averaged uniqueness theorem in its link-count form, PROVED for a spin system (`reduction/theorem_u.md` §7).** The difficulty §30's plan glossed: in the disagreement coupling the single-site influences are read at HYBRIDS of the two coupled configurations, and a hybrid of two good environments need not be good; goodness must be a property a hybrid INHERITS. The link count is one: with N_x(ω) the number of aligned links in the ball of radius r₀ around x (class angle within θ₀), ρ_x(K) the worst row over environments with N_x ≤ K, and the three inputs (U1′) ρ_x(K) ≤ 1 − η, (U2′) μ(N_{x_i} > K/2 for all i) ≤ εⁿ at mutual distance > 2r₀ for every Gibbs measure of the RP class, (U3′) 2ε(N + 1)e^a + (1 − η)e^a < 1 — THEOREM U′: the class has one element and clusters at rate a. Lemma H (a hybrid's aligned links are σ's or σ′'s, so N(ζ) ≤ N(σ) + N(σ′) and both ≤ K/2 give every hybrid ≤ K), Lemma U-A′ (van den Berg–Maes' sequential maximal coupling with the hybrids kept, the supremum over them refused), Lemma U-B′ (badness defined on the two MARGINALS, which are Gibbs, so (U2′) prices it at 2ⁿεⁿ and no hybrid is ever priced), Lemma U-C′ (the marked path sum: a good step pays (1 − η)e^a of its entropy, a mark 2ε(N + 1)e^a, Kotecký–Preiss with the marks as polymers) and the theorem are proved as stated for a finite-range specification on a compact spin space with a positive continuous density.

**31.3 — AND PROVED VACUOUS FOR A GAUGE THEORY (`theorem_u.md` §8; law 48 at a theorem: a filter every member passes is no filter).** (V1) THE ROW IS A CLASS FUNCTION OF THE ENVIRONMENT'S ORBIT: for a gauge-covariant specification the map ω′ ↦ ω′^g is a bijection of {ω′ = ω off y} onto {ω″ = ω^g off y}, and total variation is invariant under one bijection applied to both laws, so ρ_x(ω^g) = ρ_x(ω) — the row depends on the environment only through its holonomies. (V2) EVERY ORBIT HAS A REPRESENTATIVE WITH AT MOST THE HAAR-TYPICAL COUNT: fix a spanning tree; the gauge group acts freely and transitively on the tree links' values; under Haar tree values every link of the representative is Haar (a cycle link is a tree path's product times the orbit's fixed holonomy), so the aligned count in any ball has mean p(θ₀)|B| exactly for EVERY orbit, and some representative sits at or below it. CONSEQUENCE: (U2′) with ε < 1/2 needs K/2 above the count's typical value, so K > p|B|; at every such K every orbit meets {N_x ≤ K} and ρ_x(K) is the unconditional Dobrushin row — which AD read as 1.1639 > 1 at a_c = 4a. The link count carries no information about what the row depends on; §30's plan for the two numbers is withdrawn as ill-posed, not as refuted by a reading. WHAT A GAUGE-CORRECT FORM NEEDS AND WHY EACH CANDIDATE FAILS: the good property must be an orbit property (V1) and hybrid-inherited (Lemma H): aligned-PLAQUETTE counts are orbit properties and fail inheritance at the hybrid's seam (a plaquette with two links from each configuration carries U₁^σU₂^σU₃^{σ′}U₄^{σ′}, neither's; the straddling count is bounded only by 24b·|D ∩ N(x)|, uncontrolled where the disagreement is dense); a complete gauge fixing (the comb tree) makes the count an orbit function but puts the "ball" event on Wilson lines back to the fixing planes, which no chessboard estimate spreads, and the temporal gauge's residual freedom realigns ∝ r³ links against a fluctuation ∝ r²; a pair property is what the coupling uses and is not priceable, a hybrid's law under the coupling not being Gibbs. THE OBJECT THIS LEAVES — THE STRADDLING LEMMA: under the PRODUCT coupling of two Gibbs samples a straddling plaquette is EXACTLY Haar (each half-plaquette is a two-link path, jointly Haar under any gauge-invariant measure, and the two are independent), so the plaquette form of Lemma H holds in expectation with the straddling term at its Haar rate; under the sequential MAXIMAL coupling the two samples are correlated on the disagreement set, and whether its straddling holonomies stay within δ of Haar is the lemma — with it Theorem U″ (the plaquette form, (U1″) at the orbit-level count, the straddling term priced by δ) follows by §7's proof verbatim. Its proof needs the coupling's joint law on D, which van den Berg–Maes fix only through the marginals: the coarse sector's honest frontier tonight. And the premise's reading: the row at environments whose holonomies are GIBBS-TYPICAL at β = 2.2 — neither ordered nor Haar (the plaquette angle's mode at 2.2 is ≈ 100°, cos(θ/2) = (√(1 + β²) − 1)/β = 0.644; ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ ≈ 0.57) — which no instrument has read: AD's "typical" 0.78–0.82 were Haar-random environments, its 1.1639 the ordered one. The deterministic proxy an exact engine takes with no Monte Carlo: an environment whose every plaquette carries the typical angle in a fixed pattern (uniform, and staggered), the row read at both.

**31.4 The fibre's open core quantified in one convention (`reduction/fibre_theorem.md` §5; a first draft compared the coarse plaquette's flux with a per-fine-plaquette threshold and was withdrawn before landing).** In M8's additive exponential coordinates per unit β (U = e^{iA^aσ^a}, the charged pair of charge 2 under the colour-3 photon, f the photon's plaquette angle): tooth 1's record (line 8) gives the fine plaquette's flux variance 0.5/β and the 2 × 2 coarse plaquette's 1.369312/β (β_eff/β = 0.730294), so at β = 2.2 the fine field has rms 0.4767, the coarse plaquette's flux rms 0.7889, the BLOCK-AVERAGED field per fine plaquette rms 0.1972, and the fibre's own photon fluctuation rms 0.434; CVX's certificate puts the Nielsen–Olesen threshold of the block-constrained fibre at a constant abelian background in f_c ∈ (0.5890, 0.6872) per fine plaquette (L = 2, (8, 8, 2, 2), stable at every quantum n ≤ 6, unstable from n = 7; without the constraint every quantised field unstable at −2f). The background the charged modes see at Gaussian order is the lifted coarse configuration — 0.1972 against 0.589–0.687, a factor 3.0–3.5 below the threshold, exceeded at 2.99–3.49 σ (0.28 %–0.05 % of coarse plaquettes); the pointwise fine field exceeds f_c on 15 %–22 % of fine plaquettes, but that field is background plus the fibre's own fluctuation, whose coupling to the charged pair is the cubic vertex — the self-interaction, not a background. So at β = 2.2 THE NIELSEN–OLESEN INSTABILITY IS NOT THE OBSTRUCTION (for backgrounds constant over the magnetic length; a random background of that rms is the residual question, at 3 σ); THE OBSTRUCTION IS THE FIBRE'S NON-CONVEXITY AT ITS OWN FLUCTUATION SCALE: CVX's bracket for the sup-norm convexity radius of a block's link angles is (1/16, 0.40133) against a typical rms ≈ 0.35 and a typical block maximum over 64 links ≈ 0.9–1.0. By the free scaling θ ∝ β^{−1/2} the typical block maximum falls under the bracket's top at β ≈ 11 and under its certified bottom at β ≈ 460: THE BAND'S UPPER END, IN THE FIBRE'S SUP-NORM ACCOUNTING, LIES BETWEEN β ≈ 11 AND β ≈ 460 AND IS SET BY THE CERTIFIED CONVEXITY RADIUS — sharpening the certificate from 1/16 toward 0.40 moves it by a factor ≈ 41 in β (the UV program's floors 95.18 and 191.83 are the same accounting from the other side). The background crosses the threshold at 2 σ only at β ≈ 1.0, the strong end, where the cluster expansion carries the fibre. What (ii) must do at 2.2: cluster the fibre on its TYPICAL set under a sub-threshold background — a large-field/small-field split inside the fibre (Bałaban's, at the block scale), or the no-small-parameter road (RP and chessboard on the fibre's periodic measure; Lemma RP-B for centred odd L; the half-open L = 2 case open); the measurement side unchanged (fine–fine connected correlations given block averages at block distances 1 and 2 against 1.7627), the prediction now a self-coupling-sized departure, not an instability.

**31.7 The criterion's place in THE ONE FLOW; the move to odd L computed, then withdrawn by a lemma; the next instrument staked (2026-11-02 16:3x EST, read from `date`; `theorem_u.md` §§9–10; `photon_l3.awk` and `photon_l3.2026-11-02a.txt`, 7b5cf6b28).** (V3) THE ORDERED ENVIRONMENT IS THE WORST ROW, AND AT 2.2 IT IS TYPICAL. AD read 1.1639 at the aligned datum against 0.78–0.82 at Haar data: a coarse link given aligned neighbours is sharply peaked and most sensitive to them — the vacuum is the worst case, as for every ferromagnet below its Dobrushin temperature; at β = 2.2 the measure sits halfway to order (⟨½ tr U_p⟩ ≈ 0.57; cos(θ/2) = 0.644 at the plaquette angle's mode), so the large-row environments carry O(1) weight, not e^{−f|B|}: NO RARE-BAD-SET CRITERION HOLDS AT 2.2 IN ANY GAUGE-CORRECT FORM, and the Ising plus phase (a small averaged influence, two Gibbs measures) shows an averaged row proves nothing without one. In the fibre at fixed coarse c the environment is COMMON to both coupled chains, so a goodness on c alone (31.4's sub-threshold field, exceeded at 3 σ, priced by chessboard on MIR Lemma 2.1) is inherited by every hybrid — Theorem U_f is well-posed and not vacuous — but its (F1) is a supremum over the neighbouring blocks' fine configurations at the FINE coupling, the band's own failure one scale down. Every sup-based criterion fails in the band at every scale by construction; every averaged one is false without a priced bad set; the band is exactly the set of scales where neither Theorem 8.7's expansion nor the UV floors' has a small parameter.
THE L = 3 PRICE, COMPUTED (`photon_l3.awk`: photon_gauss.awk's functions verbatim; the known answers n = 1 → 0.500000001 and n = 2 → 1.369311537 against the record's 1.369312 met): the n × n flux variance per unit β reads 2.305193063 at n = 3 and 5.203604764 at n = 6, so at β = 2.2 the L = 3 block-averaged field per fine plaquette has rms 0.1137 against CVX's L = 3 constant-field threshold (0.2327, 0.3103) — a factor 2.0–2.7 below, exceeded at 2.05–2.73 σ (4.1 %–0.6 % of coarse plaquettes) against L = 2's 3.0–3.5 (0.28 %–0.05 %): the threshold falls with the block mass g_L = 2 − 2cos(π/L) (2 → 1) faster than the averaged field (0.197 → 0.114); the two-step L = 3 background 0.0427 against an extrapolated 0.3·g_6 ≈ 0.08 at β ≈ 1.8; the certified sup-norm radius 0.0350036 against a block maximum over 324 links ≈ 1.1–1.2 puts the sup-norm upper end near β ≈ 2,000–2,600; the gains would be the drift −0.4082 a step (five steps 2.2 → 0.2452) and κ₃ = 1.5106.
THEN THE MOVE IS WITHDRAWN BY A LEMMA — the record checked before landing: MIR Lemma 2.1 proves the straight-path blocked measure reflection positive at L = 2, and its §2 Remark names why (the half-open PARTITION is not reflection-covariant; the straight-path coarse VARIABLES are). LEMMA RP-F (PROVED, `theorem_u.md` §10): for straight-path blocking at ANY L and a coarse datum c fixed by the antilinear reflection θ in a coarse site-plane (c_{θℓ} = c_ℓ†), the fibre μ(· | B = c) is reflection positive. Proof: condition through nonnegative kernels in the Hilbert–Schmidt distance — mirror-invariant, since the mirror of a temporal average is its adjoint and the adjoint of an average is the average of the adjoints; the product of kernels splits as P₀ · G · θ(G) with P₀ the plane's θ-fixed factors; E[θ(F)·F·∏k] = E[θ(F√P₀G)·(F√P₀G)] ≥ 0 by Osterwalder–Seiler's reflection positivity of μ; divide by E[∏k] > 0 and let the kernels' width go to zero (the conditional law has a positive continuous density, MIR 2.1). So the fibre at every symmetric c — the vacuum and CVX's constant abelian backgrounds among them, a (1,2)-plane field being fixed by a reflection in x₀ — has a positive self-adjoint transfer operator between slices, MF's Theorem 3.1 its Gaussian case; L = 2 STAYS THE FRAME'S BLOCK, and the state table's row "half-open L = 2 NOT covered" is answered for the fibre at symmetric data. At strength: RP-F is structure, not a mechanism — a lower bound on the fibre's gap needs an upper bound on its second eigenvalue, an expansion's job; at a non-symmetric c the slice operators vary along the flow direction and their product is not symmetric.
WHAT SURVIVES IN THE BAND is an expansion with a small parameter, and the two in sight at 2.2 are the sub-threshold background (a factor three) and the coupling of an EXACTLY computed block interior to a Gaussian exterior in the block-averaged norm — UNMEASURED, because no exact engine of the campaign imposes the straight-path constraint: the constraint is a delta on an AVERAGE of path holonomies, not on a group element, and CVX's block constraint is at Hessian order. THE NEXT INSTRUMENT, STAKED BEFORE IT EXISTS — the constrained exact integrator: two adjacent 2⁴ blocks, the coarse datum c symmetric, the exterior at Gaussian order or a datum, the straight-path constraint imposed either through nonnegative kernels k_ε(‖B_ℓ − c_ℓ‖) that couple pairs of the L³ paths of each coarse link (a two-path interaction entering the character expansion beside the plaquettes, ε → 0 read by extrapolation) or through the delta's Fourier variable λ_ℓ on the quaternion space of averages (four real variables per coarse link, thirty-two for two blocks — out of reach as a grid, so the kernel road is the one to build). KNOWN ANSWERS before any reading: (i) at large β the constrained Gaussian two-block correlation of MF's Theorem G at the same geometry, the rate 1.762747 between the blocks' interior plaquettes; (ii) with the constraint removed EI's corrected two-block W4L2 moments to every digit; (iii) the datum rotated at a vertex, the reading unmoved. STAKES: the fibre's fine–fine connected correlation between the two blocks' interior plaquettes at β = 2.2 and c = 1 (the vacuum fibre) UNGUESSED between the Gaussian e^{−2·1.7627} ≈ 0.03 and the unconstrained Wilson value at the same distance; the SIGN of its departure from the Gaussian UNGUESSED; the same at c = the constant abelian background of one quantum, UNGUESSED. Kill-switches: (ii) failing means the constraint road is mis-wired; (i) failing at β = 50 means the ε → 0 extrapolation is under-resolved. The build is the trunk's own, by hand, in C and awk under the house standard; its first act after the gawk runs of record land.

**31.8 The constrained exact integrator — the blueprint, and its first two known answers built in the house language (2026-11-02 16:4x–16:5x EST, read from `date`; `reduction/cei_blueprint.md`; `photon_carried/cei_gauss.awk` with `cei_gauss.d1.txt`, `cei_gauss.d7.txt`).** THE MATHEMATICS, EXACT: E[F | B = c] = lim_{ε→0} E[F ∏_ℓ k_ε(B_ℓ − c_ℓ)] / E[∏_ℓ k_ε(B_ℓ − c_ℓ)] with k_ε(X) = e^{−‖X‖²/2ε} and ‖X‖² = ½ tr(X†X), the ℝ⁴ norm of the quaternion average (the limit is the conditional expectation because the law of B has a positive continuous density, MIR Lemma 2.1). Expanding the square for one coarse link with its eight paths, up to constants that cancel in the ratio, k_ε = ∏_p e^{a·½ tr(c†U_p)} · ∏_{p<p'} e^{−b·½ tr(U_p†U_{p'})} with a = 1/(4ε), b = 1/(64ε): eight LINE factors — the SU(2) Wilson weight at coupling a of the rotated path, e^{a·½ tr W} = Σ_j w_j(a) χ_j(W) with w_j(a) = 2(2j+1) I_{2j+1}(a)/a, CONFIRMED at the desk by class-angle quadrature at a = 1.25 (j = 0: 1.208450269345 on both roads; j = ½: 0.710459 against I₂(1.25) = 0.2220179 → 0.71046; the first gawk call had passed I_{4j+1} and was corrected by hand) — and twenty-eight PAIR factors per coarse link at the NEGATIVE coupling −b of two parallel OPEN paths, w_j(−b) = (−1)^{2j} w_j(b): the half-integer spins enter with a minus sign, a truncation is signed (EI's W3 lesson), the full expansion positive. The cap from w_j(x) ~ e^{−(2j+1)²/2x}: ε = 0.2 → a = 1.25, J ≈ 2; ε = 0.05 → J ≈ 4; ε = 0.01 → J ≈ 9; the pair couplings sixteen times smaller at L = 2. The Haar integration over the 128 fine links of plaquette characters, rotated open lines and open pairs is the Peter–Weyl bookkeeping of EI's engines extended to open objects in the interior; the ε ladder 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.025, 0.0125 read by Richardson (R(ε) = E[F | B = c] + ε R₁ + O(ε²)), two caps at every rung. THE KNOWN ANSWERS BEFORE ANY READING: (K1) the coefficients — done; (K2) the normalisation at β = 0, where a coarse link's eight paths are independent Haar quaternions and E[∏k_ε] at c = 1 is a radial Bessel integral (the characteristic function of the uniform law on S³ is 2J₁(t)/t, that of the mean of eight is its eighth power at t/8); (K3) no constraint, ε → ∞: EI's corrected two-block W4L2 class moments to every digit; (K5) the exterior datum rotated at a face vertex, the reading unmoved; and (K4) THE GAUSSIAN FIBRE, BUILT TONIGHT — `cei_gauss.awk`: the Gaussian field on the two blocks' 128 links with the exterior at the ordered datum, the quadratic form over the 360 plaquettes touching the region, the eight straight-path constraints at Gaussian order (each block's sixteen μ-links sum to zero), the constrained covariance read from the KKT system [[P + δG, Cᵀ], [C, 0]]. THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN FIRST FINDING: the fine gauge mode at the shared-face vertex is NOT a symmetry of the constrained problem — it rotates block 1's endpoint and block 2's start and moves two constraints — while the two mid-path modes are; only the latter are gauge-fixed, and the result is δ-independent to twelve digits (δ = 1 and 7; the first draft fixed all three and read a 1e-7 drift, the trap caught by the control). READINGS per unit β: the interior (0,1)-plaquette variance 0.466288191412 (unconstrained 0.466885114408); the block-distance-1 same-orientation covariance −0.011117009841 (unconstrained −0.011418539588, ratio 0.9736); the (2,3) and (1,2) pairs +0.007054513412 (0.9957); the in-block (2,3) neighbours 0.055233; the connected ½ tr correlation at Gaussian order, (3/2)(cov/β)²: 7.4e-8 at β = 50, 1.8e-8 at 100, 3.83e-5 at 2.2 as the REFERENCE. What this says about 31.7's stake: AT THIS GEOMETRY THE CONSTRAINT MOVES THE GAUSSIAN CORRELATION BY 2.6 % — the ordered datum outside confines the region more than the block constraint does — so the exact engine's discriminating content on the datum object is the NON-GAUSSIAN DEPARTURE at β = 2.2 from the Gaussian value at the same geometry (UNGUESSED in sign and size against 3.83e-5), and the fibre's own rate, MF's 1.762747, shows only on the Gaussian-EXTERIOR variant (the exterior integrated, every block's average constrained), the object the engine must grow into after the datum object; (K3) and (K4) remain the kill-switches. The price and the build order are on the blueprint page: `cei_coeff.awk` (done at the desk), `cei_haar.awk` (K2), `cei_gauss.awk` (K4, done), `cei.c` the engine on EI's state representation with the line and pair weights as open objects, `cei_gate.sh` with five teeth; the first act after the gawk runs of record land.

**31.9 The kernel's mass at β = 0 finds the vacuum datum on the boundary; the pair wall and the group-valued coarse variable; the path-count ladder on the Gaussian fibre; EI's second report (2026-11-02 16:5x–17:0x EST, read from `date`; `photon_carried/cei_haar.awk` and `cei_haar.2026-11-02a.txt`; `cei_gauss.awk` with `cei_gauss.p8.txt`, `p4`, `p2`, `p1`; `math/EI/WORK.md` §14).** (K2) BUILT, AND IT REFUTED ITS OWN SET-UP TWICE BEFORE IT SPOKE: the J₁ series was garbage past x ≈ 25 (a control read 7.8e10) and was replaced by Hankel's expansion beyond x = 20 (J₁(1), J₁(10), J₁(30), J₁(50) to the tables' digits, the seam checked); the one-point control was ill-posed (the uniform law on S³ has no density in ℝ⁴) and was dropped. THE FACT: the density ρ₈ of the mean of eight unit quaternions VANISHES at |c| = 1 — eight unit vectors average to a unit vector only when all coincide — so the exact vacuum datum c = 1 lies on the BOUNDARY of the coarse variable's range and the fibre at c = 1 is degenerate (every path equal). The record: E[k_ε(B − c)]/(2πε)² at the identity's direction, r = 1: 0.0427, 0.0546, 0.0327, 0.0080, 0.00088, 5.9e-5, 3.0e-6 as ε = 0.4 … 0.00625 (→ 0); at r = 0.9: 0.0532, 0.0822, 0.0674, 0.0262, 0.0054, 0.00079, 0.00011 (still falling: the mean of eight Haar quaternions has E|B|² = 1/8, typical norm ≈ 0.35, and r = 0.9 is deep in its tail). So the fibre's datum must sit at the TYPICAL NORM under the Gibbs measure, c = r·1 with r read by the engine from ⟨|B|²⟩ = (1/64)Σ_{p,p'}⟨½ tr(U_p†U_{p'})⟩ in the datum's gauge (at Gaussian order r → 1 as β → ∞, which is why MF's and cei_gauss's linearised constraints never saw it); 31.7's and 31.8's "c = 1" are RESTATED at c = r·1, and CVX's constant backgrounds (linearised constraints) are unaffected.
THE PAIR WALL. The average's kernel carries twenty-eight open PAIR objects per coarse link (31.8), and in any sweep order a coarse link's eight paths stay open together until the sweep passes them, so the pairs multiply the state by up to (2J+1)^{2·28}: the exact engine for the straight-path AVERAGE is out of reach at every ε of the ladder (EI's ordcost tool prices orders exactly; the plain two-block W4 object already peaks at 55⁵ entries, below). The Hubbard–Stratonovich road trades the pairs for a four-dimensional λ per coarse link — thirty-two dimensions, no grid. What has no pair terms is a GROUP-VALUED coarse variable: for U ∈ SU(2), ‖U − c‖² = 2 − 2·½ tr(c†U), so the Gaussian kernel IS the Wilson weight e^{a·½ tr(c†U)} alone — one open line per coarse link, and the degenerate boundary of (K2) disappears with it (the range is the whole group). The single straight path from the block's base vertex is such a variable; the k paths' average (k = 2: one pair; k = 4: six) is the ladder from it toward the frame's average. THE PATH-COUNT LADDER ON THE GAUSSIAN FIBRE (`cei_gauss.awk -v PATHS=k`; k = 8 reproduces the landed record byte for byte): the constrained/unconstrained ratio of the block-distance-1 (0,1) covariance reads 0.9736 / 0.7451 / 0.6311 / 0.4554 at k = 8 / 4 / 2 / 1, the interior (0,1)-plaquette variance 0.9987 / 0.9336 / 0.8797 / 0.7804, the in-block (2,3) neighbours 0.9982 / 0.9978 / 0.9989 / 0.7560, the cross-block (2,3) pair 0.9957 / 0.9972 / 1.0011 / 0.9633 — the single-path fibre is MORE constrained at Gaussian order (its constraint lands on the plaquettes the path touches) and the same kind of object: massive at the block scale, its distance-1 correlation halved. The instrument's second finding, in passing: WHICH GAUGE MODES ARE SYMMETRIES OF THE CONSTRAINED PROBLEM DEPENDS ON THE CONSTRAINT — the shared-face mode moves two constraints at k = 8 and none below (the base paths avoid that vertex), so the reader now fixes exactly the modes that move no constraint (δ-independent to twelve digits at every k) and refuses a singular system by a relative pivot. THE EXACT ENGINE'S OBJECT IS THEREFORE THE SINGLE-PATH FIBRE — EI's corrected engine plus eight open lines of length 2 at coupling a = 1/(4ε) with c = r·1 at the typical norm, feasible at W2 and W4 — and the ladder k = 1, 2 (one pair), 4 (six pairs) is read exactly where affordable and at Gaussian order throughout, the frame's average approached from below; THE ONE FLOW's chain (Theorem G, the drift, CVX's thresholds) is re-read on the single-path blocking before any exact reading is compared with it. The blueprint's §9 records this; its stakes are restated for the single-path fibre: the exact distance-1 correlation at β = 2.2 and c = r·1 UNGUESSED against the Gaussian value at k = 1 (the ratio 0.4554 times the unconstrained −0.011419, −0.005200 per unit β → (3/2)(cov/β)² = 8.4e-6 at 2.2), the sign of the departure UNGUESSED.
EI'S SECOND REPORT (resumed on its own named object at 16:0x; finished 16:5x; gate GREEN as before): ITEM 1 — PROVED BY EXHAUSTION at n = 1, 2, 3, 4 that no order of the corrected bent object's links keeps at most n plaquette-or-corner cycles open (K = n unreachable, 78,431 sets exhausted at n = 4; with the corners off K = n is reached at every n; with each corner's two links as one step it stays unreachable — the obstruction is the row transition the corners force, not their wires; at n = 2 the exact minimum over all 22! orders equals the corner order's peak at every class; general n OPEN), so every order's two-step W4 peak is at least 55⁵ = 503,284,375 entries (8.05 GB), the L1 ladder 32.2 GB a state, C_ω's L2 dump 652 GB: THE NEAR-STRIP ORDER DOES NOT EXIST and the positive cap above W2 at two steps is out of this host's reach (≥ 32 GB with the loop chains sliced, or a contraction that is not a linear sweep). ITEM 2 — the cancelling W4L4 cell priced (least peak 103,984,375 entries, 1.66 GB; 3.4 GB and 60 min measured on the ordered cell) and left. ITEM 3 — THE U(1) TWO-STEP ROWS ON THE CORRECTED OBJECTS (a new ladder mode behind a door, byte-identical to the old engine with the door unset; the kill-switch met at n = 2 to every printed digit on 671 of 672 links): 1.2 ordered 3.232306 (landed 3.231184, one → two ×1.240, rises), 0.9 ordered 3.172358 (3.162885, ×0.929), 1.2 cancelling 3.334266 (3.309469, ×0.892), 0.9 cancelling 2.589485 (2.581561, ×0.713) — the corners move them by 0.03–0.75 %, every [PG] statement at page lines 164, 182, 228 SURVIVES IN KIND, nothing withdrawn; and the U(1) two-step TV LOWER BOUNDS 1.616 / 1.586 / 2.265 / 1.627 against SU(2)'s 0.193 / 0.280 at 2.2 — a two-step reading on which the groups PART, stated as what it is: lower bounds on one side, the one-step parting of §26 withdrawn in 31.5, nothing certified. EI's next at the desk: the row-transition lemma for all n. No agent runs for the trunk; none is launched.

**31.10 The exact engines read FACE fibres; the line-cap wall; the substitution road, exact at ε = 0 (2026-11-02 17:1x EST, read from `date`; `cei_blueprint.md` §10, after reading w2e.c's cycle machinery whole).** EI's engine is a strip sweep over a two-dimensional face object — two adjacent coarse n × n loops with the bulk at the datum — not a four-dimensional block: the state is the tensor over the open wires of the cycles (plaquettes, the two loop chains, the corner cycles with their datum multipliers) cut by the sweep, and a 2⁴ block's plaquettes cut by any sweep leave tens of cycles open, beyond any cap. So what the campaign's exact engines read is the FACE FIBRE — the face's fine plaquettes given the face's coarse links, the straight paths of n fine links along the loops' edges — which is 31.9's single-path fibre restricted to a face with the bulk at the datum, and a legitimate first exact reading of a constrained fibre at β = 2.2. THE KERNEL ROAD ON A FACE: a line cycle (the path's n links as steps, the constant multiplier c† folded into the last step, the closing weight (s+1)I_{s+1}(a)/I₁(a) in the engine's own normalisation) is the corner mechanism with a constant multiplier — a small change to w2e.c — but a line stays open across the whole sweep of its face (its links lie one per column) and conditioning needs a = 1/(4ε) large, whose coefficients fall only past s ≈ √(2a·ln(1/tol)): at a = 2.5 (ε = 0.1) the cap is s ≤ 5 (dimension 6, a factor 36 per open line, 4.6e4 for three), at a = 25 (ε = 0.01) s ≈ 9. The kernel road reaches ε ≈ 0.2–0.4 — a weak tilt — and the extrapolation to zero from there is long: THE LINE-CAP WALL. THE SUBSTITUTION ROAD, EXACT AT ε = 0: the delta δ(U_path, c) integrates the path's last link out, U_n := (U₁ ⋯ U_{n−1})⁻¹c, so every cycle holding U_n holds instead the inverses of the path's other links with the constant multiplier c — the corner mechanism again, with no spin cap on the constraint at all and one link fewer to integrate per coarse link (a loop whose edge is the path collapses that edge to c, as it must). Its price is in the SLOTS and the wires: a path's first link then sits in its own cycles and in the eliminated link's — up to eight against the engine's four-slot link tensors — and the cycles holding it close later in the sweep; EI's `ordcost` (the state size exactly as w2e.c allocates it, the exhaustive minimum over orders at n ≤ 2) prices it once its cycle table is given the substituted object: UNPRICED TONIGHT, the next act. The known answers and stakes of 31.8–31.9 carry over to the face fibre: (K3) at no substitution EI's records to every digit; (K4) a face version of `cei_gauss.awk` on EI's eigeom geometry (to be built); the observable the connected correlation of a fine plaquette of face A with one of face B given the seven coarse links at c = r·1, UNGUESSED against its Gaussian value. The scope is the Clay statement; the face fibre is the first rung the house's exact engines can reach, not the last.

**31.11 The substitution road PRICED, exactly, over every order — the face fibre at n = 2 is in this host's reach at W2 and W3 (2026-11-02 17:2x EST, read from `date`; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/ordsub.c` built from EI's `ordcost.c` by `ordsub_build.sh` with the two fragments `ordsub_frag.c`, `ordsub_call.c`; the record `ordsub.2026-11-02a.txt`).** The instrument: EI's order-cost tool — the state size exactly as w2e.c's sweep allocates it, a wire open iff exactly one of its steps is processed, the exhaustive minimum of the peak over EVERY order of the links (2²² sets at n = 2) — given the SUBSTITUTED object: with ORD_SUBST=1 each of the seven coarse edges (the two loops' straight paths of n fine links, the shared edge once) has its last link in loop A's step order eliminated, every cycle holding that link holding instead the edge's other links, the eliminated links leaving the link set; the cost model needs no change, since it already prices a cycle that holds one link at two steps. THE PRICE (n = 2, one step, the loops' holonomies fixed by the substituted edges — read at loop spin 0, the loop cycles being constants of the fibre — and at loop spin 1 for the record):

| cap | plain object, least peak over every order | SUBSTITUTED, least peak over every order | factor | at loop spin 1: plain → substituted |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W2 (spins ≤ 1) | 2,744 | 537,824 (8.6 MB) | 196 | 43,904 → 8,605,184 (138 MB) |
| W3 (spins ≤ 3/2, a signed weight) | 27,000 | 24,300,000 (0.39 GB) | 900 | 432,000 → 388,800,000 (6.2 GB) |
| W4 (spins ≤ 2; the squared W2 weight lives here, positive) | 166,375 = 55³ | 503,284,375 = 55⁵ (8.05 GB) | 3,025 = 55² | 2,662,000 → 8,052,550,000 |

and the reachability search says why: with the substitution no order keeps fewer than FIVE plaquette-or-corner cycles open (K = 4 UNREACHABLE, K = 5 REACHED), against three for the plain object — the seven fixed edges tie each face's plaquettes to the corner cycles through the eliminated links' replacements, two more cycles at the best order, (Σ_s d_s²)² = 55² at W4. So THE SINGLE-PATH FACE FIBRE AT n = 2 IS COMPUTABLE EXACTLY — exact in ε, the delta on every coarse edge imposed by substitution — on this host at W2 (8.6 MB) and at W3 (0.39 GB), and at W4 needs 8.05 GB (the same 55⁵ EI met for the two-step object; this host has about 7.5 GB free: one slice short). THE ENGINE, DESIGNED ON THE PRICE: w2e.c with (i) the substitution as the corner mechanism with a CONSTANT right multiplier (the step on the eliminated link becoming a step on the edge's other link, inverted, times c = r·1 at the typical norm), (ii) THE LOOP CYCLES DROPPED — every edge's product is fixed, the loops are constants of the fibre, and dropping them keeps every link within the engine's four slots (a link then sits in its own two face plaquettes, its corner, and the eliminated link's face plaquette — four; with the loops kept the substituted links would need five) — and (iii) PER-PLAQUETTE SOURCE WEIGHTS for the fine observables: a plaquette tilted by χ₁ has the closing weights FAC′[s] = FAC[s−1] + FAC[s+1], so ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ = Z′_p/Z and the connected correlation of two face plaquettes is Z″_{pq}/Z − (Z′_p/Z)(Z′_q/Z) from four sweeps. KNOWN ANSWERS: (K3) with no substitution and no source the (0,0) class equals EI's records to every digit; (K-src) at the FREE datum (staples off, no substitution) every face plaquette's ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ is the single-plaquette Bessel ratio I₂(β)/I₁(β) exactly, because a 2 × 2 face with free boundary admits no spin above zero on any plaquette (the boundary links' Haar integrals), two-sided against a plaquette whose boundary is tied (the ordered datum) where it is not; (K4-face) the Gaussian face fibre, a face version of `cei_gauss.awk` on the same geometry at β = 50; (K5) the datum rotated at a vertex, the reading unmoved. The stakes stand as in 31.9–31.10: the connected correlation of a face-A plaquette with a face-B plaquette given the seven coarse edges at c = r·1, β = 2.2, W2 then W3 — UNGUESSED in size and in the sign of its departure from the Gaussian face value. The build is the next act; the memory gate's rerun and the records' shipment run beside it.

**31.12 THE FACE FIBRE READ EXACTLY AT β = 2.2 — the engine built on EI's, its known answers met, the first exact constrained readings beside their Gaussian (2026-11-02 17:3x–17:5x EST, read from `date`; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/`: `cei_decl.c`, `cei_funcs.c`, `cei_driver.c`, `cei_insert.awk`, `cei_build.sh` → `cei_face.c` (the engine of record, generated from EI's `w2e.c`); `cei_gauss_face.awk`; the orders `best_W2_subst.ord`, `best_W3_subst.ord` from ordsub; the records `k3_*.txt`, `ksrc*.txt`, `subst_*.txt`, `drop_*.txt`, `cei_gauss_face.2026-11-02a.txt`).** THE ENGINE: EI's corrected w2e.c with three doors — CEI_SUBST=1 substitutes the seven coarse edges (each edge's last link in loop order eliminated: a step on it with the path's sign becomes a step on the partner with the inverse sign and the constant c BEFORE its datum multipliers, a step against the path's sign a step on the partner with c⁻¹ folded into the preceding step AFTER its own; the eliminated link's staples become the partner's inverse staples read at U_e = (U_p^{−sp} c)^{se}; the loop cycles dropped; the wires renumbered), CEI_DROPLOOPS=1 drops the loops alone, CEI_SRC="p,q" tilts face plaquettes by χ₁ (closing weights FAC[s−1] + FAC[s+1]) so that ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ = Z′_p/2Z and the connected pair is Z″/4Z − Z′_pZ′_q/4Z² from four sweeps; CEI_ORDER takes ordsub's best order. THE KNOWN ANSWERS, ALL MET: (K3) with every door unset the output is EI's binary's byte for byte up to timings (36 lines); (K3′) with the loops dropped Z = 2.36286940964674839e+33 against the record's W2-Z 2.362869409647e+33; (K-src) at the free datum every face plaquette reads ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ = 0.4644790252704202 against I₂(2.2)/I₁(2.2) = 0.464479025270420, two plaquettes' connected correlation 0 to 1e-16 — and at the ordered datum 0.6391 with a connected 1.49e-3, the tied side; (K5, two-sided) the datum rotated at the MID-PATH vertex (1,0,0,0) leaves the substituted reading unmoved to 1e-16 (0.6812446854590812 → …813) while the same rotation at the COARSE vertex (2,0,0,0) moves it (0.681200) — the fibre's residual gauge symmetry is exactly the mid-path one; the state's peak 537,824 entries and five open cycles EQUAL ordsub's exhaustive prediction (the cost model and the engine agree to the entry); the quadrature rule's degree, blind to the inverse staples in the first build (a 7.6e-9 drift at the mid-path control caught it), now counts them (degree 24, 4,225 points), and the reading moved in its sixth digit. THE GAUSSIAN FACE FIBRE (`cei_gauss_face.awk`: the 22 object links, 105 plaquettes holding one, the seven single-path constraints at Gaussian order, the KKT block; no vertex has all its links in the object, so no gauge fixing): the (A0, B4) cross-face covariance per unit β −0.048763 constrained against −0.098975 unconstrained (ratio 0.49), the plaquette variance 0.3532 against 0.4566.

What the numbers say, at strength: (1) THE CONSTRAINT BITES — fixing the seven coarse edges cuts the cross-face connected correlation by 3× (squared weight) to 5× (direct) at the ordered datum and by 70× at the cancelling one, and FLIPS ITS SIGN at the random datum; the Gaussian order predicts 4× at the ordered datum. (2) THE FIBRE IS LESS ORDERED THAN ITS GAUSSIAN: 1 − ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ reads 0.319 (direct) and 0.378 (squared) against the Gaussian 0.241 — a departure of 32–57 %, the O(1) non-Gaussianity 31.4 foresaw, now a number. (3) THE CAP IS NOT YET CONVERGED: the direct and squared W2 weights part by 3.4× on the connected correlation and by 0.06 on the plaquette, the same size as the Gaussian departure, so no departure is CERTIFIED at W2; the W3 direct run (24.3 M entries, ordsub's best order, in flight) and the next cap decide — the ladder's convergence is the stake, UNGUESSED. (4) The unconstrained direct random and the W3 rows land as the chain finishes. The object is a face fibre with the bulk at a datum (31.10): what it reads is the fine fluctuation of a face given its coarse links under three exteriors, not the 4D block's fibre; the Gaussian-exterior variant and the block fibre remain the next objects, and THE ONE FLOW's chain is re-read on the single-path blocking before any of these readings is set beside it. No claim about the gap is made from a reading; the scope is the Clay statement.
*31.12 RESULTS, PART 1 (2026-11-02 18:0x EST, read from `date`; `subst_W3_ordered.txt`; the random-datum records re-written to colon-free names, a colon in a name on NTFS being an alternate data stream — the first III commit of this section refused the pathspec and was redone).* THE W3 DIRECT CAP AT THE ORDERED DATUM (24,300,000 entries at the peak — ordsub's exhaustive prediction again to the entry; five open cycles; 157 s a sweep): ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ = 0.746505, the connected cross-face correlation 5.934e-4. SO READING (2) ABOVE IS CORRECTED (law 18): the departure from the Gaussian SHRINKS WITH THE CAP — at W3 the plaquette stands 1.7 % under its Gaussian value (1 − ⟨½ tr⟩: 0.2535 against 0.2408, a 5 % departure where W2 read 32 %) and the connected correlation 20 % under it (5.93e-4 against 7.37e-4, where W2 direct read 2.6× under and W2 squared 30 % over). The W2 figures were the truncation's. What stands: the constraint's bite (the connected correlation 5.9e-4 constrained against the W3 unconstrained value, in flight) and a fibre within a few per cent of its Gaussian on the plaquette at the deepest cap this host reaches — the ladder W2 → W3 moved the plaquette by +0.065 and the W4 cap (8 GB, the positive squared weight at spins ≤ 4) is one slice beyond this host, so the convergence is read, not certified; the random-datum W3 row and the unconstrained W3 row follow as the chain finishes. The unconstrained direct W2 at the random datum, now recorded: ⟨½ tr⟩ 0.476877 with the squared weight (31.12's table) and the direct value in `drop_W2_random1.txt`.

*31.12 RESULTS, PART 2 — THE W3 CHAIN COMPLETE AND THE EXTERIOR'S INFLUENCE (2026-11-02 18:1x EST, read from `date`; `subst_W3_ordered.txt`, `subst_W3_random1.txt`, `drop_W3_ordered.txt`, `subst_W2_random{1..5}.txt`, `subst_W2sq_random{1..5}.txt`, `drop_W2_random{1..5}.txt`).* THE W3 ROWS: at the ordered datum the UNCONSTRAINED face object reads ⟨½ tr U_p⟩ = 0.688355 with the connected cross-face correlation 2.436e-3, the CONSTRAINED fibre 0.746505 with 5.934e-4; the Gaussian face fibre reads 0.688660 / 3.036e-3 unconstrained and 0.759185 / 7.369e-4 constrained. So at the deepest cap this host reaches: the unconstrained plaquette agrees with its Gaussian to 3e-4 (0.05 %), the constrained one to 1.7 %, THE CONSTRAINT'S BITE — the constrained-to-unconstrained ratio of the connected correlation — reads 0.244 exact against 0.243 Gaussian, and the constrained connected correlation itself sits 20 % under its Gaussian (5.93e-4 against 7.37e-4; the unconstrained 2.44e-3 against 3.04e-3, also 20 % under). AT STRENGTH: W3 is the signed direct truncation (EI's fix 2), W2 → W3 moved the plaquette by +0.065 and the connected correlation by 2.1×, and the W4 cap (the positive squared weight at spins ≤ 4, 55⁵ entries, 8.05 GB) is beyond this host's 7.5 GB and the engine's own 4e8-entry ceiling — the agreement is READ at W3, not certified, and a Gaussian-order formula agreeing to 3e-4 with a truncated non-perturbative sum at β = 2.2 on an object pinned by an ordered exterior is a cancellation to be checked at the next cap before it is believed; what the three caps say together is that the face fibre at the ordered exterior is within a few per cent of its Gaussian on the plaquette and within 20 % on the connected correlation, with the constraint's bite ratio the best-converged number (0.19 → 0.24 across W2 direct → W3 direct, the Gaussian 0.243). THE FOUR LEADS OF 18:3x (the operator's message; each weighed against the record before any code). One correction of the message's reading first: at W3 the constrained connected correlation is 20 % SMALLER than its Gaussian (5.93e-4 against 7.37e-4), read at one block distance — not "decayed 20 % faster", which would need two distances. (1) THE GAUSSIAN EXTERIOR THROUGH THE KKT SCHUR COMPLEMENT — worthy in its correct form, not as stated: the exterior links enter the engine only through the staple weights, and if the exterior were a PRODUCT of independent Gaussian links each staple's character coefficients would indeed be damped by one factor at zero state cost; but the constrained Gaussian exterior's Schur complement onto the object's boundary is a FULL quadratic form in the boundary links' angles (the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map), and its off-diagonal part is a pair interaction between boundary links — 31.9's pair wall again, 22² pairs. What is exact at zero cost is the DIAGONAL of that form: a per-link exterior self-energy e^{−β S_ii(1 − ½ tr U_i)} on every boundary link, a Wilson-like weight at coupling βS_ii that the engine takes as a staple with the identity multiplier; the off-diagonal S_ij is the priced remainder. DECIDED: build it — S from the free exterior's Green function (photon_gauss.awk's G4 is in the house; the constrained exterior's block transform next), the diagonal as self-energy weights (`CEI_SELF`), the reading at the Gaussian-typical exterior between the ordered and the Haar ones, the off-diagonal norm printed beside it. (2) THE W4 CAP BY SLICING — worthy, exact and cheap: the engine's FIXV already clamps a wire for a whole sweep (the junction mechanism, "a clamped wire enters the link tensor as that one slice"), so clamping the spin and the two open wires of ONE cycle at the peak and summing the 55 slices outside is an exact rearrangement of the sum that takes the peak from 55⁵ to 55⁴ (146 MB) — at a cost of nothing but time (≈ 220 sweeps for the four source configurations, hours in the background). DECIDED: built first, tonight, after the engine's gate finishes bending its fragments (`CEI_PEAK` lists the open wires at the peak, `CEI_CLAMP` loops the slices; the W2 sliced sum must equal the unsliced Z to 1e-12 before W4 is read); it certifies the W2 → W3 → W4 convergence on the POSITIVE squared weight. (4) THE PERFECT COVER INTEGRATED OUT — the decimated potential φ(|w|) = log(2I₁(|w|)/|w|) is globally convex IN w by Turán's inequality (Lemma 8.6), as the message says, but w_a = βΣ_p S_p(a) is a nonlinear function of the L₀ links and the fibre's convexity is in THEIR angles: Hess φ(w(θ)) = φ″(∂w)² + φ′ ∂²w, and the second term is the staple map's curvature — the same BCH non-convexity, weighted now by φ′(|w|)/|w|, which falls like 1/|w|. DECIDED: the decimated Hessian on V is added to CVX's engine as a reading (the trunk's own build on `cvx.c`, no agent), stake UNGUESSED whether r_c rises; its exact-engine form folded into (3). The order of attack stands: (2) tonight, (1) next, (3) the instrument, (4) a reading on CVX's engine.

**31.14 The exterior read exactly at Gaussian order: the diagonal model drops more than it keeps, the datum average is the controlled form; the clamp; the house instruments' two defects (2026-11-02 18:5x–20:xx EST, read from `date`; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/cei_dtn.c`, `cei_pairs.awk`, `cei_eig.awk`, `cei_cub.awk`, `cei_cub_run.sh`, the engine's fragments; `photon_carried/photon_gauss.awk`).** The correction the side agent made at 18:5x stands and is sharpened here by the reader's own numbers. THE DIRICHLET-TO-NEUMANN READER (`cei_dtn.c`): the free photon's plaquette form `P` on a box of radius `R` round the two-face object, the exterior links integrated exactly at Gaussian order by the Schur complement `S = P_OO − P_OX P_XX⁺ P_XO` (conjugate gradients to 1e-12 on 466,390 exterior links at `R = 8`, 166 iterations at most); three known answers two-sided — `S` symmetric to 1.6e-13, every diagonal fell (the exterior can only soften), and every gauge mode of the fifteen object vertices lies in `S`'s kernel to 1.7e-13 (at `R = 0`, where the box cuts the vertices' links, the same check reads 4.5 — the control fires); the readings converge in the box like the massless response (link 0: 2.25978, 2.25659, 2.25621, 2.25611 at `R = 2, 4, 6, 8`). THE KEPT FORM: the engine keeps the face plaquettes exactly and the corner plaquettes as cycles, so the exterior's whole effect on a link beyond those is `R = S − P_kept`: its diagonal is an effective staple count `κ_i = S_ii − n_face − n_corner` in place of the ordered datum's `n_stap` (0.256 on the four corner-adjacent links against 4, 1.115 on the ten five-staple links, 1.364 and 1.614 on the shared and interior links against 4 — the Gaussian exterior relieves the pinning three- to sixteen-fold), and **its off-diagonal is larger than its diagonal: `‖offdiag R‖_F = 8.29` against `‖diag R‖_F = 5.48` (ratio 1.51), spread over the 231 pairs with the largest share 3.6 % and the top forty carrying 73 % (`cei_pairs.awk`)** — so the diagonal self-energy model is not an approximation of the Gaussian exterior with a known error, and no handful of pair cycles would make it one. Its readings are therefore LABELLED POINTS between the two exteriors the engine already reads, and are recorded as such: at W2 the ordered datum 0.6812 / 2.813e-4, the diagonal Gaussian-typical model 0.6115 / 1.377e-3 (`CEI_SELF`, the per-link weight `e^{κ_i β(½ tr U_i − 1)}` in characters to `KSELF`, an eliminated link's riding on its partner's value; the κ = 1 weight equals the staple weight at the rule's 2,541 points to 0.0 — the same Bessel ratios; the cap moved the reading 4e-7), the Haar exterior with the corners at the datum 0.5176 / **0 to 5.6e-17** (with no staple on any link the two faces' plaquettes decorrelate exactly); at W3 the diagonal model 0.6250 / 2.145e-3 against the ordered 0.7465 / 5.934e-4. **THE CONTROLLED FORM IS THE DATUM AVERAGE.** Integrating the exterior is averaging the engine's reading over the exterior's own marginal on the engine's 168 outside links — the engine is a datum reader, and the reading is invariant under gauge transformations at every vertex (Haar invariance of the object's measure at the object's vertices, the exterior measure's invariance elsewhere), so the marginal may be taken in any gauge: `cei_dtn.c marg` builds the exterior-only form (the plaquettes with no object link — the straddling ones the engine carries whole) plus the Feynman term (the squared divergence at every vertex; the Faddeev–Popov determinant is a constant for a linear gauge), positive definite, and reads the covariance `(4/β) F⁻¹` on the 168 links by 168 CG solves (symmetric to 5.7e-14, positive definite, trace 68.22, the spectrum flat — half the trace in 51 modes, 90 % in 136 — per-colour variances 0.32–0.73 against the bulk Feynman value 0.28, since the links beside the object lack the straddling plaquettes' pinning). The average is a degree-3 Gaussian cubature (Stroud's 2n points at `±√n` along an orthonormal basis of the `n = 504` field dimensions, weight `1/2n`; the rule's second moment 204.672 = 3 tr Cov) in two rules sharing no point — A along the eigenbasis, B along the canonical basis through the symmetric square root — their difference the rule's own error at degree four; each point a datum file the engine reads (`file:<path>`, round-trip known answers: the ordered listing re-read reproduces the pinned reading to the last digit, a Haar datum dumped and re-read to 1e-16); the reading `Σ w Z_p / 2 Σ w Z_0` with the importance diagnostics printed. The two rules' readings are UNGUESSED on this page; the runs (1,008 points each at W2) are in flight. THE CLAMP (lead 2): one corner cycle (cycle 10, open from step 4 to 11, the only cycle open at both peaks) with its spin and both wires fixed, the 55 slices summed outside — an exact rearrangement that never lets the cycle enter the state (its closing weight applied once per slice): at W2 the 14 slices reproduce the unsliced `Z` to 2.6e-16 and the reading to 1e-16 at a peak of 38,416 = 537,824/14; the four source configurations now branch at the first source closing (step 8 of 15, the sweep shared to there); the W4 positive-weight reading at the ordered datum runs at a peak of 55⁴ (UNGUESSED; the W3 ordered reading 0.746505 / 5.934e-4 is what it will be read against). THE HOUSE INSTRUMENTS' TWO DEFECTS, FOUND BY THEIR RUNS OF RECORD: tooth 2's gawk engine (`su2_remainder.awk`) finished at N = 32 and N = 48 and equals the Python anchor's rows to every printed digit (D(0.785) = −0.254698, D(1.571) = −0.251888 at N = 48); the decider's `[house]` T2 fit needs the N ≥ 64 rows (eight rows in the anchor's three records) that the gawk engine cannot reach in a night — a C port is the named instrument, and until it runs the anchor's T2 PASS (D0 = −0.25972 from 8 rows, stake −0.257512) stands on rows the house engine has verified at N ≤ 48. THE READING AT W2, THE ORDERED DATUM, between face 0's far column and face f's far column (separation `2f + 1` fine units): connected 1.758210e-7 (sep 3), 1.131917e-10 (5), 7.283063e-14 (7), then the roundoff floor 1e-16 (9, 11, 13, 15, 17); the one-point value 0.68182556 at every K to 1e-10 from K = 4 on; **the decay factor per coarse cell is 1553.3 then 1554.2 — a constant, so the fibre's transfer operator along the strip at this datum has a dominant gap `ln 1553 = 7.35` per coarse cell, 3.68 per fine unit** (the fibre at the ordered datum is a strongly pinned system: every link under four or five identity staples); and the whole sweep at W2 runs at a peak of 14⁴ = 38,416 entries at every K, so the strip reaches W3 (30⁴) and W4 (55⁴ = 9,150,625) at every separation in minutes — both in flight, UNGUESSED, with the Haar-random data at W2 beside them (the fibre's gap at a typical exterior is the object Theorem X (ii) is about; the ordered datum's is its best case). **31.15 RESULTS, PART 1 (20:3x–20:5x EST): W3 along the strip; THE TRANSFER OPERATOR EXTRACTED, AND ITS SPECTRUM IS SORTED BY THE REPRESENTATION THROUGH THE CUT.** At W3 the strip reads 5.308936e-7 / 3.100444e-10 / 1.808553e-13 at separations 3 / 5 / 7 (then the floor) — the factor per cell 1712.4 then 1714.3, the one-point value 0.7482086 — so the cap moves the gap from 7.35 to 7.45 per cell; at W4 the first cell reads 7.080233e-7 (sep 3), the rest in flight. THE OPERATOR ITSELF (`CEI_TRANSFER=iA:iB:cshift:wshift`): the strip's sweep is periodic cell by cell (196 → 196 → 2744 → 38,416 → 2744 → 196 → 196 entries at W2), and at the cut after each cell's x-links exactly two plaquette cycles are open with two wires each — a 196-dimensional cut space at W2 (900 at W3, 3025 at W4); every basis vector of the cut after step 19 is injected and carried through the next cell's seven surviving links to the cut after step 29, read as the translate (cycles +4, wires +16; the engine checks every output cycle and wire is a translate) — T is 196 × 196 at W2, built in 1.8 s, 900 × 900 at W3 in 512 s (`transfer_W2.matrix.txt`, `transfer_W3.matrix.txt`); not symmetric in the wire basis (‖T − Tᵗ‖/‖T‖ = 0.32), as a reflected cell is not the cell. THE SPECTRUM, by power iteration and a Wielandt deflation chain, each mode with its weight in the sweep's own cut state and in the cut state carrying the χ₁ source on plaquette 2: **W2 — λ₁ = 3.6097e18; a TRIPLET at λ₁/51.7518 with weights 5e-14 to 2e-13 in both physical states; a SINGLET at λ₁/1552.96 with weights 2.9e-7 (sweep) and 3.0e-6 (source) — the two-point function's own factor (1553.3 / 1554.2); a QUINTUPLET at λ₁/4354.58, weights ≤ 3e-12. W3 — the triplet at 56.8548 (weights ≤ 3e-13), the singlet at 1712.04 (3.1e-7 / 5.0e-6; the two-point 1712.4 / 1714.3), the quintuplet at 4384.71.** The degeneracies are the SU(2) irreducibles carried through the cut by the residual gauge rotation at the coarse edge's mid-path vertex (K5-MID's symmetry): the charged sectors (adjoint, spin-2) have modes slower than the physical one — the triplet decays thirty times more slowly than the singlet — and NO physical state reaches them (their weights sit at roundoff), so the clustering of gauge-invariant observables is governed by the singlet sector's gap, ln 1553 = 7.35 per cell at W2 and ln 1712 = 7.45 at W3, and the certified tier must restrict T to the singlet sector before any inertia test — the sector is the intertwiner space of the cut (the note's second weapon in its exact place). The chain's degenerate members converge slowly (one quintuplet member reached the iteration cap with its eigenvalue right to 1e-12): a block method is the next instrument there.

THE GAP OVER DATA (`transfer_scan_W2`): the transfer operator extracted at the ordered, the cancelling and twenty Haar-random data at W2 — ordered and cancelling 1552.96 (the cancelling datum touches the first shared link's staples, outside the extracted cell); **at the Haar data the physical gap is 30.88 (random:1) and 21.61 (random:2)** — mode 2, reached by the physical states, since a random datum breaks the ordered datum's global SU(2) and the modes no longer sort into irreducibles — **fifty to seventy times smaller than at the ordered datum**: ln 21.6 = 3.07 per cell, 1.54 per fine unit at W2. **31.16 The three instruments proposed at 20:5x, weighed against the record (21:1x EST).** (1) "The fibre's integer spectrum is the exact constant of the two-step contraction on every finite carrier; the Gaussian fibre is already the exact lower bound." Two refutations at the desk and one correction: the staggered pole and the integer spectrum are the BLOCK-AVERAGED Gaussian fibre's (Ο1, Addendum I, S-B1), while the engine's fibre is the holonomy map's (31.12), whose transfer operator has a positive leading spectrum and no staggering; the holonomy-constrained Gaussian fibre of the strip decays faster, not slower, than the non-abelian one at the ordered datum (2329 against 1781 per cell); and at a Haar datum the non-abelian fibre's factor per cell, 21.6, is below the averaged-map Gaussian's e^{2κ} = 34 at cosh κ = 3 — so neither Gaussian is a lower bound on the rate over coarse configurations. The INSTRUMENT the proposal names is retained in its exact place: the cell's transfer operator in the character basis, its characteristic polynomial by ΤΟΜΕΥΣ' leap (Hessenberg modulo verified primes, Garner), restricted to the singlet sector at the ordered datum and taken whole at a random one, under Lemma RP-F — the certified tier of 31.15; the constant it certifies will be the minimum over data, not a Gaussian number. (2) "The measure-weighted datum by the exact integrator, the Schur complement's diagonal as a self-energy, the off-diagonal the priced remainder." Built before the proposal arrived and priced: the diagonal is not a controlled approximation (31.14 — the remainder exceeds it), and the datum average over the exterior's marginal has an integrand spanning forty-three orders of magnitude, so no fixed cubature of it converges (the rule's effective points will be read when its run ends); the age and the skeleton price a rational integrator's steps and stop it, which this integrand is not — the controlled form is the DRESSED exterior: the object's own quadratic response along the exterior's eigenmodes (168 second differences, in flight) folded into the marginal, the residual weights of order one, and the cubature on that measure — built as the next rule. (3) "The ultraviolet half by the same leap on Bałaban's sequence, the interface as the fibre's residue, γ the number at which the certificate first passes." Not transportable as stated: the blocked weights of the sequence are not character expansions with known coefficients, so no exact characteristic polynomial exists to read; the open pieces of Lemma C (the uniform analyticity domain, CMP 116 Lemma 1's bounds through the second background derivative, the continuity in the earlier couplings) are inductive estimates on the effective actions, and γ "defined as where the certificate first passes" is a definition that moves the question into the certificate's hypotheses. What the system can do on that half is finite: the blocked transfer operator of the fibre at successive block scales on explicit lattices at weak coupling (the strip's cell operator at β large, where the Gaussian comparison is exact to the order read), checking that the fibre's rate approaches the Gaussian rate from below as the coupling falls — a check of the inductive bounds' conclusion at the fibre, not their proof. The assembly stands where the record marks it: the strip's gap at the minimum over data, read at its cap, is the fibre clause's measured content at the face level; the 4D block fibre (lead 3) and the coarse row's certificate remain the open objects, and existence remains unverified here.
