**31.15 RESULTS, PART 5 — THE CERTIFIED TIER'S FIRST TWO STONES; THE CAP RAISES THE DISORDERED GAP (22:0x–22:1x EST, read from `date`; `cei_exact_haar.awk`, `cei_exact_bessel.awk`, their records).** THE HAAR LAW EXACT: the normalised Haar measure of SU(2) is the uniform measure on S³ in the quaternion coordinates, and every integrand the engine integrates is a polynomial in them (a character is the Chebyshev polynomial U_s(w) in w = ½ tr U; a D-matrix entry in the monomial basis of Sym^s is a polynomial of degree s with coefficients in ℤ[i]; a staple weight at a datum T is a polynomial through ½ tr(UT)); the monomial law `∫ w^a x^b y^c z^d dμ = ∏ (2kᵢ)!/(4^{kᵢ} kᵢ!) / (K + 1)!` (zero unless every exponent is even) is run in exact rationals — the moments 1/4, 1/8, 1/24, 5/64, 1/192 met, the character orthogonality `∫ χ_s χ_t = δ_st` met at all 81 pairs to spin 8, a bent χ₃ parting (4/1, SIGHTED). THE WILSON COEFFICIENTS CERTIFIED: `I_n(11/5)` as exact rational partial sums of sixty terms with the tail under the geometric majorant — enclosures of width 1e-165 — so `c₁ = 0.928958050540840574…`, `c₂ = 0.466478043979525581…`, `c₃ = 0.1616323047925…`, `c₄ = 0.0427711121212…` are certified rationals; the engine's free-datum known answer `I₂/I₁ = 0.4644790252704` lies INSIDE its enclosure and OUTSIDE the enclosure at a bent β = 12/5 (SIGHTED); and **the cap's tail bound** `Σ_{k>K} (k+1)² I_{k+1}/I₁ ≤ 0.929, 0.283, 0.0688, 0.0139` at K = 2, 3, 4, 5 — the sup-norm bound on one weight's character truncation (|χ_k| ≤ k + 1), the quantity the certified tier's truncation estimate is built from (crude when compounded over a cell's forty weight factors; the positive squared weight of fix 2 is where it sharpens). With these two stones every entry of the cell's transfer operator is an exact rational combination of certified rationals in the monomial basis; the exact contraction of a cell (the third stone) is the next build, and its known answer is the floating operator's spectrum (λ₁ = 3.6097197687738e18, the singlet at λ₁/1552.958844 at W2). THE CAP AT A DISORDERED EXTERIOR: at random:1 the cell gap reads 25.20 at W3 against 7.89 at W2 — the character cap moves the disordered gaps far more than the ordered one (1553 → 1712 there), so the W2 scan's minimum 6.9 is a low-cap reading and the W3 and W4 values at the slow data are the ones to carry; the other two W3 gaps, the second and the dressed cubature rules, the W5 strip, the W4 bent object and tooth 1's retaken record are in flight.

**31.14 CORRECTION, DATED (22:2x EST, read from `date`): THE CUBATURE OF THE EXTERIOR AVERAGE IS WITHDRAWN AS AN INSTRUMENT — ITS POINTS ARE NOT THE EXTERIOR'S.** RESULTS 3's sentence "the exterior's Gaussian fluctuations move the two-face reading by 0.05 % and 0.5 %, with 161 effective points" is withdrawn (law 18): a degree-3 Stroud rule on the field's n = 504 dimensions puts every one of its 1,008 points at √n = 22.4 standard deviations along ONE mode and at zero along the other 503, so no point of the rule is a configuration the Gaussian marginal ever produces (a typical field has every mode near one deviation); the rule integrates polynomials of degree three exactly and the engine's Z is an exponential of the field, so its "average" is a weighted sum over extreme one-mode excursions — the 161 effective points are the modes whose excursion barely disturbs the object, not representative samples — and the number 0.680872 measures nothing it was labelled with. The same geometry is rule B's and the dressed rule C's, both stopped at 22:1x (311 and 106 points in, their partial records kept as what they are: engine readings at extreme one-mode data). The side note of 22:1x found this; the trunk had let the dynamic-range warning of 31.14's own first reading be answered by the effective-point count, which measures the weights' spread and not the points' typicality. What stands: the datum readings themselves (ordered, Haar, the one-mode excursions) as readings at their data; the exterior's Gaussian marginal (`cei_marg.R8.txt`), exact in its model; the object's response along the exterior's modes (`response_W2`), exact at second order. What the exterior average needs, said plainly: the exterior is not a parameter to be averaged over from outside — it is part of the system, and the engine already integrates it where the object is enlarged to contain it: the strip did so along one direction (the gap scan over Haar data is the honest reading of how the transverse exterior varies the fibre, 6.9 to 293 per cell at W2), and the 4D block fibre (lead 3) does so whole. No fixed low-degree rule on a 504-dimensional Gaussian will average an exponential integrand; the road through the exterior's marginal is closed here and the roads through larger objects are the ones kept.

**31.17 The fibre clause's roads, weighed; the gap is not a function of the pinning (22:2x–22:4x EST, read from `date`; `pinning_scan.2026-11-02a.txt`; S1 §§23–25 read whole).** What Theorem X (ii) needs is the fibre's clustering uniformly in the coarse datum, in 4D, at β = 2.2. Three roads, each at its strength. (1) THE GAS ROAD (lead 3's calculus, S1 §§23–25): the fibre's weight is a product of Wilson weights under the single-path constraints, its character expansion a gas of closed spin surfaces with sources at the coarse edges, and Theorem V's vertex-norm bound, Theorem L's local bound and Lemma R's realigned operator-norm bound price every polymer by local star integrals — bounds that are DATUM-UNIFORM BY CONSTRUCTION, since a character's modulus is at most its dimension whatever the exterior's values multiply its argument; but the gas converges only where the surface count is beaten, at strong coupling (the full gas's 0.2452; the fibre's edges being constrained removes link integrals and adds none, so its threshold is of the same size), and at β = 2.2 the fibre is in its ordered regime, where this expansion diverges. The gas road proves the uniform clause where the whole theory is already gapped, and not at the crossing. (2) THE SADDLE ROAD (the certified tier): at any datum the fibre is a small-fluctuation system round the datum's own minimum (every link under four or five staples), its cell transfer operator extracted and, in the monomial basis with the two stones of RESULTS 5, exact; under Lemma RP-F it is a positive operator at a reflection-symmetric datum and ΟΛΟΚΛΗΡΩΣΙΣ Ο6's inertia door certifies its gap — AT THAT DATUM. The uniform clause then needs a bound over the datum space SU(2)^{~40} of one cell's staples, which no cover reaches, so it needs a structural lemma: the gap as a function of a few invariants of the datum. (3) THE POSITIVITY ROAD (Birkhoff–Jentzsch): the exact cell kernel in the group variables is positive and bounded, 1 ≥ K ≥ e^{−2β N_p} with N_p the plaquette factors in a cell's kernel, so its Hilbert-metric contraction gives a gap uniform in the datum — of size e^{−2β N_p}, some 10^{−38} per cell at N_p ≈ 40, true and useless, and in 4D the cross-section's plaquette count enters N_p and the bound dies with the volume. THE INVARIANT ROAD (2) NEEDS, TESTED AND REFUTED AT FIRST ASKING: the cell's seven surviving links' pinning — the norm of the sum of a link's staple group elements at the datum, 5 at the identity, 0 when they cancel — was put beside the twenty Haar gaps (the engine's `CEI_PIN`): the weakest link's pinning ranges 0.73–1.87 and the mean 2.15–3.24 across the data while the gaps range 6.9–293, and the rank correlations are **ρ(gap, weakest pinning) = −0.25, ρ(gap, mean pinning) = +0.05** — the gap at a disordered exterior is NOT a function of how strongly its links are pinned (random:8's 293 and random:3's 6.9 have the same weakest pinning, 0.98 and 0.92); it is a function of the staples' ORIENTATIONS relative to one another and to the coarse constraint — the exterior's local holonomy, not its strength. So the structural lemma the uniform clause needs is about the datum's local flux through the cell, and the night's instruments give its first table (twenty rows of gap, and the datum files that made them). This is the open object of the fibre clause at the face level, stated as such; the 4D block carries the same question with the same engines to build.

**31.15 RESULTS, PART 6 — THE SPECTRUM READ WHOLE: THE HAAR-DATUM GAP COLUMN CORRECTED FROM RAYLEIGH READINGS TO EIGENVALUES, THE PINNING RE-RUN, THE DATUM IDENTITY, THE THIRD AND FOURTH STONES (22:3x–23:2x EST, read from `date`; `cei_spectrum.c`, `cei_gap_reader.awk`, `spectra_W2.2026-11-02a.txt`, `pinning_rerun.2026-11-02a.txt`, `cei_exact_link.awk`, `cei_exact_rep.awk`; landed 35133a003, e5ca5b5be).** THE CORRECTION, DATED (law 18; three defects in one column, found in this order by the advisor's reading of the raw record): (1) RESULTS 4's rule took the first mode whose weight in the sweep's cut state passed the threshold; at every Haar datum the Wielandt chain's members past the first sat at the 5000-step cap, so they come out in no order and the first physical mode is not the slowest — at W3 random:2 the chain reported mode 2 at 53.5 while mode 5 carried weight 0.76 at 15.2. (2) The driver's printed ratio λ₁/λ is real/real; at a Haar datum the members are complex with im/re up to 1.7 (the ordered datum's are 1e-13), and the decay rate is the modulus. (3) The chain cannot converge there at all: T is non-normal (‖T − Tᵗ‖_F/‖T‖_F = 0.32 at the ordered datum) and, as the spectrum now shows, every Haar datum's second eigenvalue is a complex-conjugate pair of equal modulus — random:3 alone has a real negative λ₂, and there the chain had converged, reading 6.902275 exactly — which power iteration cannot separate. The reader's own convergence flag never fired (a greedy regex ate the step count, law 16) and its summary's counter was awk's own NR (the §4 builtin trap: "38 of 3"), both caught by a planted self-test the first draft had passed by coincidence (9-ter). So the Haar-datum column of RESULTS 2–4 ("6.9–293, geometric mean 41.4"; the W3 "25.2 / 53.5") was a RAYLEIGH READING — the quotient of an unconverged mixture, inside the hull of the spectrum it mixes — and §31.17's pinning correlation was computed on it. THE EIGENSOLVER: `cei_spectrum.c`, Householder reduction to Hessenberg form and the shifted QR iteration (Wilkinson shift from the trailing 2×2, Givens steps on the active block, an exceptional shift every tenth step), complex throughout, no library — the textbook algorithm (Francis 1961; Golub–Van Loan 7.5), W = 0. Its known answers: five planted spectra met (±i; (5 ± √33)/2; the fourth roots of unity; the companion matrix of (x−1)…(x−5)) and a bent entry parting (SIGHTED); on the ordered W2 matrix the top ten moduli equal the chain's converged members digit for digit — 3.609719768774e18; 6.975065477680e16 (×3); 2.324414315771e15; 8.289475025996e14 (×5) — with the trace identity Σλ = tr T to 1.3e-15, 566 QR steps, 0.3 s. The 22 W2 scan data were re-run with CEI_TRANSFER_OUT (`transfer_dumps.2026-11-02a.txt`) and each 196×196 matrix read whole, every trace check at or below 7e-15. THE READING, |λ₁|/|λ₂| over the whole spectrum (at a Haar datum no symmetry sector exists, so every mode is physical): the twenty Haar data read 9.05, 10.50, 6.90, 8.88, 17.38, 28.75, 40.26, 29.84, 31.69, 9.80, 16.27, 13.88, 24.09, 17.70, 20.08, 10.35, 13.84, 29.49, 20.77, 27.55 — MIN 6.90 (random:3, the real staggered mode), MAX 40.26 (random:7), GEOMETRIC MEAN 17.19 (ln 2.84 per cell) — against the Rayleigh column's 6.9 / 109.5 / 26.7 by modulus and RESULTS 4's 6.9 / 293 / 41.4: the Rayleigh readings overestimated the gap by up to 3.5× (random:9, 109.5 → 31.69) and the minimum did not move, random:3 being the one datum both roads read. The ordered and cancelling data read 51.751769 over the whole spectrum (the non-singlet sector, three equal moduli) and 1552.958844 in the singlet sector (the chain's converged physical mode, the sector exact there by symmetry); every Haar datum's gap lies below the ordered datum's non-singlet gap. THE PINNING, RE-RUN on the eigenvalue gaps (`pinning_rerun.2026-11-02a.txt`): Spearman(gap, the weakest link's |Σ staples|) = −0.16, Spearman(gap, the mean) = −0.22, where the Rayleigh column had given −0.25 and +0.05 — §31.17's verdict stands with different numbers: the gap is not a function of the staples' strength. THE DATUM IDENTITY (the comparison across caps needs the same group elements): the engine's Haar datum is seeded by geometry — each outside link's quaternion is a hash of its coordinates, its direction and the seed, never of its index (cei_face.c's 05:0x note) — and the CEI_LISTOUT listing at W2 and at W3 for random:1 is byte-identical on all 248 outside links; so the W3 readings at random:1–3 are the same data at the next cap, their 900×900 matrices in flight for the eigensolver, and the W2 → W3 comparison is written when they are read, not from the Rayleigh column (RESULTS 5's sentence "the cap raises the disordered gap" was read from that column and is withdrawn until then). THE FOURTH STONE (`cei_exact_link.awk`): the staple-weighted character integrals S(n; s₁..s_m) = ∫ (1 + Σ_k c_k U_k(w))ⁿ Π_j U_{s_j}(w) dμ(U) — the scalar a link with n staples and slot spins s_j contributes at the identity datum — by two roads sharing no line, exact rationals by the monomial law with the certified c_k cut at forty digits against the class measure (2/π) sin²θ dθ at 400 midpoints and 220 bits: nine cell scalars agree to 2.4e-63; the orthogonality 25 of 25; the spin-1 slot reads c₁/2 by orthogonality (the coefficient's placement checked) and meets the engine's quadrature 4.644790252704e-01 at its 13 digits; c₂ doubled on one road alone parts them by 1.1e3 (SIGHTED). THE THIRD STONE (`cei_exact_rep.awk`, Sym^s in the monomial basis over ℤ[i]) was corrected at the desk before its record: the trace identity χ_s(U) = U_s(w) holds on the group, not as raw polynomials (it is an identity modulo w² + x² + y² + z² = 1), so it is read at five rational unit quaternions over 65 — one first test point was not a unit and was replaced — 6 of 6 spins; the homomorphism 4 of 4, unitarity 4 of 4, the D-matrix census 16 of 16, a conjugated α breaking the homomorphism at s = 2 (SIGHTED). WHAT STANDS: the fibre's cell gap at Haar data is an eigenvalue reading at W2 — never below 6.90 per cell on twenty data, geometric mean 17.19 — and its cap dependence is read on the same data when the W3 and W4 matrices are read whole; the uniform-in-datum clause still lacks its structural lemma, the gap as a function of the datum's local holonomy through the cell; the certified tier's fifth stone, the exact cell operator in the character basis with this spectrum as its known answer, is the next instrument on that road.

**31.18 THE VALIDATION PROTOCOL — THE OPERATOR'S ORIENTATION ORDER (23:0x EST, read from `date`).** This lane — the trunk seat iii-43 — is the lane the concurrent seats are oriented toward helping: the best new measurements and bug fixes come here with a message; the whole of the Yang–Mills proof, its process, engines, measurements and results, counts as valid only when this lane has verified it; and whatever a seat's own goal, the orientation is to get the perfect, ground-up version of everything into this lane and verified in Lean step by step here. Operationally: a seat lands by pathspec on main as it does now, then messages the hash; a deliverable is the object, not a summary of it (a measurement: the run of record, its gate and arm, its known answer, its sighted control, its hash; a bug fix: the defect, its site, the reading before and after, the tooth that now bleeds; a proof step: the statement at its strength, every hypothesis and where it is discharged, the finite piece a kernel can decide); this lane re-derives each item on a road it did not write, runs its gate, and folds it into the Lean chain at `LEAN/aperanton` under aperanton's gate — a finite item as a theorem proved by `decide` on the object itself, no axiom and no sorry; an analytic item as a statement with its hypotheses named and its finite pieces decided. Nothing enters the proof's state on a seat's say-so, this lane's own included. The order was sent to the three live seats at 23:0x; the first object taken under it is iii-9e's block-averaged Gaussian fibre operator (`STDLIB/build/holoklerosis/fibre_matrix.2026-11-02a.txt`: the 16×16 polynomial matrix C(z) at eight cells and the pole law cosh κ = 3 + 2n_π + m²), whose pole is to be witnessed in the kernel by an explicit vector over ℤ[√d] with C(z₀)v = 0 checked by `decide`, the determinant never computed.

## §32 — STEP 1(a) OF THE SIX STEPS: THE STRUCTURAL THEOREM — THE CELL'S EXTERIOR ENTERS THROUGH ITS GAUGE ORBIT, THE COMPLETE INVARIANTS NAMED, THE CERTIFICATE'S DOMAIN A COMPACT ORBIT SPACE; WHAT IT DOES AND DOES NOT DO (2026-11-03 02:5x–03:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat, at the desk; the operator's six-step plan of 01:3x adjusted on iii-08's and iii-9e's advice, both refusing a fitted polynomial as a lemma; the engine's gate arm 5 the known-answer instance)

**1. The object.** The strip cell C of §31.15: its link set L_C (the ten links of steps 20..29 of the K = 3 strip at W, seven surviving the substitution and three paired with an eliminated partner), its vertex set V_C, its plaquettes P_C (those with all four links in C ∪ the fixed coarse edges), and its cut, the state space 𝓗_W spanned by the open cycles' spins s ≤ W and the open wires' matrix indices. The exterior datum ω — the 248 outside links — enters the cell's Wilson weight in exactly three ways: (a) through the STAPLE SUM of each cell link ℓ = xy, S_ℓ(ω) = Σ_{p ∋ ℓ, p ∉ P_C} U_{p∖ℓ} ∈ ℍ, the sum over ℓ's staples of the ordered product of the other three links of p read as a quaternion, so that the link's weight is exp(β⟨U_ℓ, S_ℓ⟩) with ⟨·,·⟩ the inner product of ℝ⁴ and |S_ℓ| ≤ n_ℓ, the number of staples (for a paired survivor the one field S_ℓ + conj S_{ℓ′} of iii-08's correction, §31.17); (b) through the corner multipliers M_c, the products of outside links along the outside steps of the corner cycles; (c) through the substitution, which fixes the coarse edges at c = 1. Write ω ↦ (S(ω), M(ω)) for the staple-and-corner data; the cell's weight is W_ω(U) = Π_{p ∈ P_C} exp(β·½ tr U_p) · Π_{ℓ ∈ L_C} exp(β⟨U_ℓ, S_ℓ(ω)⟩) · (the corner factors), and the transfer operator T_ω on 𝓗_W is the integral of W_ω over the cell's links in the character basis with the cut's wires left open.

**2. THEOREM S (the exterior enters through its gauge orbit).** Let G_res be the residual gauge group of the cell: the maps g : V_C → SU(2) with g_x = g_y across every fixed coarse edge xy. Let G_res act on the data by S_ℓ ↦ g_x S_ℓ g_y⁻¹ for ℓ = xy and on each corner multiplier by the same rule at its endpoints.
- (i) For every gauge transformation of the lattice supported on the exterior and on V_C whose restriction to V_C lies in G_res, T_{ω^g} = U_g T_ω U_g⁻¹, where U_g is the unitary on 𝓗_W that multiplies each open wire at a cut vertex x by D^s(g_x) in its spin block; hence spec T_{ω^g} = spec T_ω.
- (ii) A gauge transformation supported on exterior vertices not in V_C leaves S(ω) and M(ω), hence T_ω, unchanged.
- (iii) Consequently the spectrum of T_ω, its gap γ(ω) = log(|λ₁|/|λ₂|) and every spectral quantity are functions of the G_res-orbit of (S(ω), M(ω)). The orbit space Ω_C = {(S, M) : |S_ℓ| ≤ n_ℓ, |M_c| = 1}/G_res is compact, and the gap is a function on it. A complete set of invariants is: the norms |S_ℓ|² and the real parts of the quaternion products along the closed walks of the cell's graph with the fixed coarse edges contracted, a link traversed backwards contributing its conjugate and a corner multiplier entering as a link — the first fundamental theorem of invariant theory for SU(2) along a graph (Procesi, *Adv. Math.* 19 (1976), for the matrix form; the lattice form, in which the gauge-invariant functions of link variables are generated by the Wilson loops, Giles, *Phys. Rev. D* 24 (1981)).
- (iv) The character truncation by spin commutes with the action of (i) — a wire transforms within its spin block — so (i)–(iii) hold for the exact operator and for every W-truncated engine alike.

*Proof.* (i) The gauge transformation is the change of variables U_ℓ ↦ g_x U_ℓ g_y⁻¹ on the cell's links, under which the Haar measure of each link is invariant (bi-invariance), the plaquette traces of P_C are invariant, and the fixed coarse edges are invariant by the hypothesis g_x = g_y across them. Its effect on a staple sum is S_ℓ ↦ g_x S_ℓ g_y⁻¹: the staple of ℓ = xy is a path from y to x through two exterior vertices, at which the factors g and g⁻¹ of consecutive links cancel, leaving the endpoints' factors; the same cancellation gives the rule for the corner multipliers. Hence W_{ω^g}(gUg⁻¹) = W_ω(U) pointwise, and the integral over the cell's links of W_{ω^g} with the wires open equals the integral of W_ω in the new variables, in which every open wire's D^s(U_ℓ) has become D^s(g_x)D^s(U_ℓ)D^s(g_y)⁻¹; the factors D^s(g) at the cut vertices are the unitary U_g (D^s is unitary on each spin block, and the cycles' spins are unchanged), so T_{ω^g} = U_g T_ω U_g⁻¹. (ii) At an exterior vertex not in V_C every staple and corner path passes through the vertex with one link in and one link out, and g⁻¹g cancels. (iii) is (i) and (ii) together with the compactness of a product of closed balls modulo a compact group acting continuously; the completeness of the invariants is the cited theorem, the fixed edges contracted because they carry the identity under the residual group. (iv) is read off the proof of (i): U_g is block-diagonal in the spins. ∎

**3. The known-answer instance, already in the gate.** `cei_gate.sh` arm 5 (run i GREEN, e5ca5b5be): the datum rotated by a gauge transformation at the MID-PATH vertex (1,0,0,0) leaves the substituted reading unmoved, |diff| < 1e-12, at c = 1 and at c = (0.6, 0, 0.8, 0) — that vertex is exterior, (ii) — while the same rotation at the COARSE vertex (2,0,0,0) moves it by more than 1e-6: that vertex is an endpoint of a fixed edge whose other endpoint is not rotated, so the transformation is not in G_res and the datum leaves the orbit. Theorem S is the statement the arm measured, with the group that makes it true named.

**4. What the theorem does.** It reduces the supremum over the exterior that Step 1's certificate must take — iii-08's k-step Dobrushin–Shlosman coefficient as a supremum over SU(2)^248 — to a supremum over the compact orbit space Ω_C, of dimension at most 4|L_C| − 3·|V_C/∼| (ten links, the vertices identified across the fixed edges), parametrised by the invariants of (iii); and it tells the instrument which readings of the datum can matter at all: only the norms and the closed-walk traces, which are the magnitudes and the preferred-holonomy traces iii-08's flux table measured, together with the walks that table did not form. It also settles one question qualitatively: for the EXACT operator of the strip cell at fixed β, T_ω has a strictly positive continuous kernel on the group variables, so by Jentzsch's theorem λ₁ is simple and strictly dominant and γ(ω) > 0 at every datum; γ is lower semicontinuous on the compact Ω_C, so min_{Ω_C} γ > 0 exists — the uniform-in-datum positivity of the strip cell's gap is a theorem, with no value attached.

**5. What it does not do, said at its strength.** (a) It attaches no number: the minimum of §4 is existence, and Hypothesis II′ needs ρ′ with its K′. (b) The strip cell is NOT the fibre: its exterior fixes the fine links outside the strip as well as the coarse data, so its gap is the rate of a further-conditioned object, an anchor for the fibre's clustering constant and not its value; the fibre of the reduction (THE GROUND, Hypothesis II′, lines 124–130) is the fine field given the coarse variables alone, a specification on the fine lattice whose clustering at the block scale is a Dobrushin–Shlosman condition over blocks with the sup over the exterior — iii-08's instrument, whose domain this theorem reduces — in the averaged currency MF's file names (the Gaussian part Theorem G, the large-field correction priced by its λ-probability). (c) The truncated engines' gaps are the truncation's as much as the physics' (iii-08's cap table: a pinned link at x = β|S| = 22 keeps 24 % of its weight at W2, 50 % at W4), so the certificate's object is the exact operator with the cap's tail bounded or the cap raised at the pinned links, which the trunk is building into the engine (per-link caps) as Step 1(c). The readings of §31.15 stay readings. Nothing above is a claim about Hypothesis II; it is the first lemma of its certificate's construction, and the sup it reduces is the one the instrument now takes.

**§32 CORRECTION — THEOREM S (i) AND (iii) WERE TOO STRONG, AND THE CONTROL THAT DECIDES THE CORRECTED FORM IS BUILT AND READ: THE EIGENVALUES ARE INVARIANT UNDER THE PERIODIC RESIDUAL GROUP ONLY, THE SINGULAR VALUES AND THE TV-TYPE QUANTITIES UNDER THE WHOLE RESIDUAL GROUP (2026-11-03 03:4x–04:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the engine's multi-vertex gauge door `CEI_GAUGES` in the cei_funcs fragment, called from the inserter's tensors anchor; `gauge_control.2026-11-03a.txt`; `cei_gram.awk`, `cei_specdiff.awk`)**

**1. What was wrong, found by reading the cut.** The dump is the operator from the cut after step 19 to the cut after step 29 READ AS ITS TRANSLATE τ (cycles +4, wires +16: CEI-TRANSFER's own line). A gauge transformation g acts on the entry cut's open wires by U_A(g) and on the exit cut's by U_B(g) = U_A(g∘τ), so T_{ω^g} = U_B(g) T_ω U_A(g)⁻¹ — a UNITARY EQUIVALENCE for every admissible g and a SIMILARITY only when U_A(g) = U_B(g). §32's (i) wrote the similarity for every g in the residual group and (iii) rested on it; both are replaced by Theorem S′ below, and §3's sentence that arm 5 "measured" the spectral statement is withdrawn: arm 5 rotated the datum and read a scalar observable, which instances the substitution's constraint and the exterior-only invariance, not the spectrum. §1 (the object), (ii) exterior-only, (iv) the truncation commuting with the wires' D^s(g), §4's Jentzsch statement and §5 stand; §4's dimension count is withdrawn (it omitted the corner multipliers and used the wrong group).

**2. The groups, read off the substituted strip.** CEI-SUBST fixes ten coarse edges at c = 1, each a straight path of two fine links with one of the two eliminated in favour of its partner (U_e = U_p⁻¹): the bottom and top rows' x-pairs and the four full rungs at x = 2, 0, −2, −4. A pair is preserved by a gauge transformation iff g agrees at the pair's two ENDPOINTS (the middle vertex cancels), and the ten coarse edges connect all eight coarse vertices (2,0), (2,2), (0,0), (0,2), (−2,0), (−2,2), (−4,0), (−4,2): so **G_res = {g : one common value on the coarse class, arbitrary at every other vertex}**. The entry cut's four open wires sit at the free vertex (−2,1) (one wire of each open cycle) and at the coarse class (the other wire of each); the exit cut's, by τ, at (−4,1) and the coarse class. Hence **G_per = {g ∈ G_res : g_{(−2,1)} = g_{(−4,1)}}** and **G_0 = {g ∈ G_res : g = 1 at (−2,1), at (−4,1) and on the coarse class}**.

**3. THEOREM S′.** (i) For g ∈ G_0: T_{ω^g} = T_ω. (i′) For g ∈ G_per: T_{ω^g} = U_g T_ω U_g⁻¹ with U_g = D^{s}(g_{(−2,1)}) and D^{s}(g_coarse) on the wires in their spin blocks, so the spectrum is invariant. (ii) For g ∈ G_res ∖ G_per: a unitary equivalence U_B T_ω U_A⁻¹ — every singular value, every unitarily invariant norm and, for the exact kernel, the total-variation distances between conditional laws (a gauge transformation is a measure-preserving bijection of the cell's variables) are invariant; the eigenvalues are not. (iii) exterior-only transformations leave T_ω unchanged (§32's proof). (iv) A transformation outside G_res — one coarse vertex alone — is not a symmetry of the substituted object; nothing need hold. (v) Consequently the spectrum of T_ω is a function of the G_per-orbit of the staple-and-corner data (S, M), and the singular values and TV-type quantities of its G_res-orbit; the orbit spaces are compact, and the orbit of a compact group acting linearly on a real vector space is determined by its polynomial invariants (the orbits are compact and disjoint; a continuous separating function is approximated by polynomials and averaged over the group), which for the gauge action on the strip's quotient graph are generated by the traces of closed walks — in the quotient with the coarse class one vertex and, for G_per, (−2,1) identified with (−4,1) — a link traversed backwards as its conjugate (the quiver invariant theory: Le Bruyn and Procesi, Trans. AMS 317 (1990), the compact form by the unitarian trick; the lattice statement for group-valued links Giles, Phys. Rev. D 24 (1981)). *Proof* as §32's, with the wires' open indices picking up D^s(g) at their vertices and the entry and exit wires identified by τ: U_B T U_A⁻¹ is a similarity iff g∘τ = g at the wire vertices, which on this strip is the one condition g_{(−2,1)} = g_{(−4,1)}, the coarse class carrying one g already. ∎

The heat-bath law of one link given its staple sum V is p_V(U) = e^{β⟨U,V⟩}/Z, Z = 2I₁(β|V|)/(β|V|); the influence of a neighbour sharing m staples is the total-variation distance between p_V and p_{V′} with |V − V′| ≤ 2m, its supremum over the move read exactly (Simpson at 200², the KAT TV(V, V) = 0 and two opposite peaks at x = 22 reading 1.000000). ## §34 — THE BLUEPRINT WEIGHED AGAINST THE RECORD BEFORE ANY CODE: WHAT IT STATES RIGHTLY, WHAT IT OVERSTATES, WHAT IT REFUTES ITSELF ON, AND THE ASSIGNMENTS (2026-11-03 04:35 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the operator's blueprint of 04:0x — Part I, YM-1a–c, YM-2A–B, YM-3 — as it reached iii-08's seat and was forwarded verbatim at 04:3x; the operator's standing order of 04:4x repeated: close every open item, no placeholders, no scope minimization; nothing above edited)

**1. The frame.** "99 % built" is struck as a measure: the open core is the band — Hypothesis I and Hypothesis II′ at O(1) coupling — and the ultraviolet threshold, and a percentage says nothing about where the difficulty sits. What the blueprint names as the three gaps is the record's own state table (31.6, 30.5): YM-1 the fibre's non-Gaussian clustering uniformly over the exterior and in 4D; YM-2 the coarse sector at 2.2; YM-3 the ultraviolet-to-crossover bridge. VERIFIED. Its account of the Gaussian fibre sector (cosh κ_n = (2n² + 1)/(n² − 1), e^{κ_n} ≥ 2 + √3 at every level, the staggered pole leading on every cell read) is T-816 and §33.4 (a): VERIFIED. Its account of diagonal reflection positivity is CORRECTED in one word: "resolved" means IN THE INFINITE-VOLUME LIMIT UNDER (H) (ΑΝΑΚΛΑΣΙΣ Addendum I Theorem C; the construction's own clustering, the same input the gap rests on, counted once), while on finite even tori it FAILS with exact certificates (PK; ΑΝΑΚΛΑΣΙΣ Lemma 2) and is not needed there, Corollary 8.3 consuming the limit's. The strong end for every compact simple G at MG2's thresholds stands with one reader; iii-9e's Ο5 per-group inputs are the second road, in progress.

**2. YM-1a — Stone 5 and the k-block split: VERIFIED as the record's own next instruments** (31.15 RESULTS 5; ΟΛΟΚΛΗΡΩΣΙΣ Addendum V; iii-08's certspec.c, §31.17 RESULTS 3). "GAP-UNCONDITIONAL at the full precision" is the target of the split, not a result in hand. ASSIGNED: iii-08 — the certspec × eig_cert k-block split first (owed to iii-9e), then `cei_exact_cell.c` (Stone 5: the seven surviving links contracted in the monomial basis with Stones 1–4, the entries certified rationals with their radii), its known answer the floating operator's spectrum (λ₁ = 3.6097197687738e18, the singlet at λ₁/1552.958844 at W2); this lane re-derives on a road it did not write.

**3. YM-1a's spin-cap tail — REFUTED AS STATED, its right tool kept.** The sentence "the Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the tail block is bounded by the two-plaquette cut tail 2 Σ_{s>W} d_s² a_s⁴, ≤ 2.4 × 10⁻⁵ at W4; by Weyl/Bauer–Fike this certifies the infinite-spin gap" carries no derivation in the record and is wrong in three places. (a) The character cap acts on EVERY weight factor of the cell — about forty, the plaquettes and the staple weights of seven links — not on the cut's two plaquettes alone; a norm bound on T's tail compounds over them, which is exactly RESULTS 5's remark ("crude when compounded over a cell's forty weight factors"). (b) "Every discarded spin sector drops a positive-definite summand" bounds the LEADING eigenvalue from below for a positive operator and says nothing about |λ₂|: the gap is a ratio and is not monotone under dropping positive summands. (c) The exponent four on a_s and the number 2.4 × 10⁻⁵ are not derived from anything on the record. What is right and is kept: Weyl's and Bauer–Fike's perturbation bounds on the similarity-split block, once the tail block's operator norm is CERTIFIED — from the certified Bessel tails of RESULTS 5 (Σ_{k>K} (k + 1)² I_{k+1}/I₁ ≤ 0.929, 0.283, 0.0688, 0.0139 at K = 2..5 per weight) compounded honestly over the cell's factors, or sharper on EI's positive squared weight where the per-weight tail is a tail of a product. ASSIGNED: the trunk, at the desk — the honest tail bound of the cell operator at W, written with its compounding and its number, before any certificate names γ_∞.

**4. YM-1b — the observable switched from the eigenvalues to the singular values and to the Birkhoff coefficient: VERIFIED in part, CORRECTED in one sentence.** Theorem S′ (ii) already states the singular values' invariance under the whole residual group, and the control of §32 CORRECTION read it (case f: the eigenvalues moved by 95 % of λ₁, every singular value held to 3e-17). The Birkhoff–Hilbert projective contraction coefficient of the EXACT positive kernel is invariant under the whole residual group too, since a gauge transformation is a measure-preserving bijection of the cell's variables and preserves the cone of positive functions — and for an INHOMOGENEOUS chain it is the multiplicative quantity (the Hilbert-metric contraction of a product is at most the product of the cells' contractions, Birkhoff): the right observable for Theorem X (ii)'s uniform clause, which §31.17's road (3) already names together with its size, e^{−2βN_p} per cell, true and useless in 4D. CORRECTED: "the singular values govern the product ⋯T_{ω₂}T_{ω₁}" — they do not; ‖T₂T₁‖ ≤ ‖T₂‖‖T₁‖ bounds the NORM of the product, and σ₁/σ₂ of each cell bounds no decay rate of the chain, because the top singular vectors rotate between cells. The chain's rate is the Birkhoff coefficient's, on the singlet-projected cone at symmetric data (Lemma RP-F) and on the full positive cone at a general datum.

**5. YM-1b's interval-box exhaustion of Ω_C — REFUTED AS PRICED.** The planar strip cell's orbit space has dimension about twenty-two (ten links' staple sums, forty real coordinates, less the residual group's eighteen at the five free vertices and the coarse class; the §32 CORRECTION withdrew the earlier count and this one is an estimate said as such), and the gap varies by a factor of fifty across twenty data; a certified branch-and-bound cover needs boxes whose Lipschitz width resolves the gap — of order 10⁻² to 10⁻³ in the staple coordinates against sides of order ten — so the count of boxes exceeds 10⁶⁰ on any accounting. §31.17 (2) priced it in one line ("a bound over the datum space SU(2)^{~40} of one cell's staples, which no cover reaches"). The method is sound in principle and useless at this dimension; the structural lemma — the gap, or better the Birkhoff coefficient of 4 above, as a function of FEW invariants of the datum — remains the object, and the flux table's silence (§31.17 RESULTS 2: |ρ| ≲ 0.3 for every local gauge-invariant reading at W2) is the measured difficulty of naming them. The blueprint's own explanation of that silence (the W2 cap; the periodic-subgroup invariance of the eigenvalue gap) is VERIFIED as the §32 CORRECTION's reading.

**§34 NOTE — THE SPIN-CAP TAIL AT THE DESK: THE HONEST BOUND AND ITS NUMBER (2026-11-03 05:11 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; §34 item 3's assignment; the instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/cei/captail.awk` (gawk, the house language), its record `captail.2026-11-03a.txt`).** THE BOUND. The cell's exact transfer operator has the kernel K(h, h′) = ∫ Π_i f_i over the cell's links with the cut's holonomies fixed, each f_i a positive factor on SU(2) — a plaquette weight e^{β·½ tr U_p} or a link's staple weight e^{β⟨U_ℓ, S_ℓ⟩} = e^{x·½ tr(U_ℓ T)} at coupling x = β|S_ℓ| — and the cap-W engine replaces every f_i by its character truncation f_i^W. Normalising each factor by its supremum (the gap λ₁/|λ₂| is scale-free), the telescoping Π f_i − Π f_i^W = Σ_i (Π_{j&lt;i} f_j^W)(f_i − f_i^W)(Π_{j&gt;i} f_j) and the Schur test against probability measures give ‖T − T_W‖_op/‖T‖ ≤ Σ_i ε_i(W) with ε_i(W) := ‖f_i − f_i^W‖_∞/‖f_i‖_∞ ≤ Σ_{k&gt;W}(k+1)² I_{k+1}(x_i) / Σ_{k≥0}(k+1)² I_{k+1}(x_i) (|χ_k| ≤ k + 1, every coefficient positive, the supremum at the identity) — RESULTS 5's per-weight tail, compounded over the cell's factors by a SUM, which is the honest form of "the tail block's norm"; Bauer–Fike then certifies the infinite-spin gap from the cap-W matrix only where that sum is small against |λ₂|/λ₁. THE NUMBERS (the record; the KAT: I₁(2.2) = 1.914095 against the table's 1.914089, I₂/I₁ = 0.464479025 the engine's digits, the plaquette tail at K = 4 reproducing RESULTS 5's 0.0688 to 2 %): the per-factor relative tail ε at character index K (spins ≤ K/2) reads, for x = 2.2 (a plaquette): 0.18, 0.054, 0.013, 0.0027, 4.6e-4 at K = 2..6 and 9.1e-6 at K = 8; for x = 11 (a link under five identity staples): 0.78, 0.61, 0.44, 0.29, 0.17 at K = 2..6, 0.049 at K = 8, 0.010 at K = 10, 2.1e-4 at K = 14; for x = 22 (a paired survivor under ten): 0.91, 0.82, 0.71, 0.59, 0.47, 0.25, 0.12, 0.044, 0.014 at K = 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14; for x = 4.4: 0.44, 0.23, 0.097, 0.036, 0.011, 8.1e-4. THE ALIGNED CELL (four plaquettes at 2.2, seven links at 11, three paired survivors at 22): the compounded tail reads 8.87, 6.95, 5.25, 3.79, 2.62 at K = 2..6, 1.11 at K = 8, 0.42 at K = 10, 0.144 at K = 12, 0.044 at K = 14 — against the non-singlet gap's |λ₂|/λ₁ = 1/51.75 = 0.0193 — so THE SUP-NORM ROUTE CERTIFIES NOTHING AT THE ALIGNED EXTERIOR BELOW K ≈ 16, where the cut state (Σ_{n ≤ K+1} n²)⁴ exceeds 10¹², and the blueprint's "2.4 × 10⁻⁵ at W4" is five orders of magnitude from the honest 5.25 (its 2Σ d_s² a_s⁴ counted two plaquettes where the cell has fourteen factors, and the squared coefficient where the sup-norm tail is linear). A HAAR EXTERIOR (ten links at staple sums of norm about 1.5, x = 3.3, four plaquettes): 3.93, 1.57, 0.52, 0.145, 0.035 at K = 2..6, 0.001 at K = 8 — against |λ₂|/λ₁ = 1/6.90 at the slowest datum: equal at K = 5 (spins ≤ 5/2, the strip's 91⁴ peak, 4 h 43 min a run) and below it by a factor four at K = 6 (140⁴ = 3.8 × 10⁸ entries, 6 GB), so at mild exteriors a POSITIVE infinite-spin gap, not its value, is certifiable by Bauer–Fike at K = 6 — exterior by exterior, since the staple sums range 0.7–3.2 (x up to 7, where the K = 6 tail is 0.05 a link). WHAT THIS SETTLES: γ_∞ cannot be read off the strip cell's finite matrices at the aligned exterior at any cap this host reaches, and at a disordered exterior only as "positive" near K = 6; the certified tier's object is therefore the exact operator's gap on a road that does not pass through a cap's tail — Jentzsch's uniform positivity (§32 §5) with a VALUE needs the kernel's bounds in the group variables, not the character basis. THE SHARPENING NAMED, NOT DONE: T is Hilbert–Schmidt, and the truncated factor's L²(Haar) tail (Σ_{k&gt;W}(k+1)² a_k²)^{1/2}, the squared coefficients, is far below its sup-norm tail — the telescoping with one L² factor and the rest in sup norm bounds ‖T − T_W‖_HS by Σ_i ε_i^{(2)}(W) with ε^{(2)} the L² relative tail; its numbers follow in captail's next column, and whether they fall under 0.0193 at the aligned exterior at a reachable cap is UNGUESSED. Every number here is the sup-norm form; nothing above certifies γ_∞ at the aligned exterior.

**§34 NOTE 2 — THE SPIN-CAP TAIL, CORRECTED AT THE DESK: THE HAAR-EXTERIOR SENTENCE WITHDRAWN, THE NORM ROUTE CLOSED AT EVERY CAP (2026-11-03 05:43 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, law 18 on its own §34 NOTE of 05:1x; nothing above edited).** THE DEFECT. The §34 NOTE compared the compounded relative tail Σ_i ε_i(W), a bound on ‖T − T_W‖_op in units of the SUP scale Π_j ‖f_j‖_∞, against |λ₂|/λ₁ — which presumes ‖T‖ = λ₁. For a product kernel of positive weights that is false by the product of the weights' sup-to-mean ratios: a weight e^{x·½ tr U} has supremum e^x and Haar mean 2I₁(x)/x, the ratio 2I₁(x)/(x e^x) ≈ 0.80 x^{−3/2} — 0.193 at x = 2.2, 0.133 at 3.3, 0.022 at 11, 0.0077 at 22 — and λ₁ is of the order of the kernel's AVERAGE while ‖T‖_op is bounded only by its supremum, so λ₁/Π‖f_j‖_∞ can be as small as the product of those ratios, 10⁻⁹ at a mild Haar exterior (ten links at x ≈ 3.3, four plaquettes) and 10⁻¹⁹ at the aligned one. Bauer–Fike needs ‖T − T_W‖ small against λ₁ − |λ₂| ≈ λ₁, and the sup-scale bound Σ_i ε_i · Π‖f_j‖_∞ is never that small at any cap in reach: THE SENTENCE "at mild exteriors a positive infinite-spin gap is certifiable by Bauer–Fike at K = 6" IS WITHDRAWN; the aligned-exterior conclusion (nothing certifiable) stands a fortiori. THE POINTWISE ROUTE FAILS TOO: a relative bound f_i^W = f_i(1 − r_i) with sup|r_i| ≤ ρ_i would give the Perron eigenvalue's enclosure directly (positive kernels, entrywise comparison), but the truncation's relative error is largest exactly where the exact weight is smallest (near U = −1, e^{−x} against a sum of large terms; the spins-to-3/2 weight is NEGATIVE there, EI's fix 2), so ρ_i is not small at any cap. WHAT STANDS: the cell's infinite-spin gap cannot be read off any finite character-basis matrix by a norm or a pointwise perturbation bound, at any exterior, at any reachable cap; the certified tier's object for γ_∞ is the EXACT operator in the group variables — its kernel strictly positive and continuous (Jentzsch's positivity, §32 §5) — with a value by a route that never passes through a cap's tail: the Birkhoff–Hopf contraction of the exact positive kernel (true and useless in 4D, §31.17 road (3)), or an exact finite-rank reduction of the kernel whose error is bounded in the KERNEL's own L² (Hilbert–Schmidt) norm against λ₁ with λ₁ bounded below by the vacuum's matrix element — the owed derivation, said here as owed and not done; the captail numbers stand as what they are, the sup-scale tails. The L² sharpening named in the NOTE is moot for the same reason and is not pursued.

CMP 116 p. 20 (read): the resummed terms' exponentials "(1 − 5δ)κd_k(Z_i) … replaced by (1 − 6δ)½Lκd_{k+1}(Z′_i)", Z′_i the smallest D_{k+1}-domain containing Z̃_i (the one-layer enlargement) — that replacement IS (2.36), applied with no additive constant; "Of course, we assume that ½(κ₁ − 1) ≥ 2Lκ"; C₃ = 2(L + 2)⁴O(1)·2E₀C₁α₄⁻¹α₆⁻¹M^q exp C₂κ₁ (α₆⁻¹ in C₃, NOTE 7's reading confirmed on the page); Lemma 3 (2.38). THE REFUTATION. Let Z be the L⁴ small cubes of one big cube C. Z̃ is the (L + 2)-cube about C, properly meeting C and its 3⁴ − 1 neighbours and nothing else, so Z′ = the 3⁴ block of big cubes about C, and d_{k+1}(Z′) = 15 EXACTLY: the sixteen corner cubes of the block (every index ±1) need sixteen distinct tree vertices (a vertex meets the cube of index −1 in a coordinate only with that coordinate on the near wall, +1 only on the far wall), two of them at distance ≥ 1 for every coordinate in which their patterns differ, so a tree holding them has ≥ 15 edges of the big grid, and the central cube's skeleton's spanning tree attains 15. d_k(Z) = δ_L, the same number for L = 3 (Z IS a 3⁴ block of small cubes) — so at L = 3 (2.36) demands 2(1 − 5δ)·15 ≥ (1 − 6δ)·3·15, i.e. 25 ≥ 36 at δ = 1/30: FALSE. iii-32 reached the same domain and the same verdict at the desk in the same hour (bab6d568c, Z = one big cube's small cubes, "2δ₃ ≥ 3δ₃: FALSE"); this lane's road was the 3⁴ block of small cubes straddling the big lattice — the same Z — with the corner argument giving the 15 exactly; the convergence between two readings blind to each other is the finding (law 40). On the chain family (m cubes in a row, d_k = m − 2, the enlargement's span m + 2 meeting j big cubes, d_{k+1} = j − 2) the house instrument finds (2.36) FALSE at L = 3 as well (m = 3: a deficit of 11/15 under the containment convention), and PASSING at every L ≥ 5; the block family passes at every L ≥ 5 by Lemma MS's bound δ_L ≥ (L⁴ − 16)/16 (53.3 against 48 at L = 5, 1080 against 84 at L = 11). THE CONVENTION FOR Z′ DECIDED: "the smallest domain containing Z̃" admits a big cube only if Z̃ meets it with positive measure — a cube Z̃ touches on a face alone is not needed to contain Z̃, that face lying in the properly-met neighbour — so the "proper" column is the page's; a tree meets a closed cube at a point. THE VACUITY THEOREM (desk): a wall-connected domain of n cubes spans at most n per axis, its enlargement at most n + 2, so for n + 2 ≤ L every big cube met shares one corner point and d_{k+1}(Z′) = 0: (2.36) holds trivially for n ≤ L − 2, i.e. for n ≤ 9 at L = 11 — the n ≤ 8 instrument named for L = 11 would decide nothing; what is undecided at L = 11 is the class of domains of span ≥ L − 1 in some axis, where the block, chain, sheet and cross families all pass by their formulas and Bałaban's statement is otherwise unproved. ## §35 THE COARSE-POINT READING, SPECIFIED — HYPOTHESIS I's ROW IN THE GROUND's SU(2)⁴-PER-SITE FORM AT THE ORDERED ENVIRONMENT (2026-11-03 06:50 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, §34 item 8; AD's page and its Lemmas A–E read; THE GROUND §8.3 and Theorem 8.5′ the form)

**35.1 THE OBJECT.** The coarse variable at a coarse site Y is the bundle V(Y) = (V₀(Y), V₁(Y), V₂(Y), V₃(Y)) ∈ SU(2)⁴ of the tree-averaged coarse links of AD's map (the axial-tree transport of every one of the n⁴ fine lines of the block to the block's centre, their sum normalised to the group; gauge covariant, codimension zero, the identity at n = 1; Lemma A: its legs are a coarse gauge transformation, so a coarse loop of V is the chain-map smoothing C²F of the flux with no perimeter self-energy), at the record's two-step scale n = 4 from β = 2.2 (a_c = 4a). The site's conditional law given the environment ξ (every other site's bundle) is the law of V(Y) under the coarse measure; the PAIR's maximal correlation is ρ(Y, Y′ | ξ) = sup E[f(V(Y)) g(V(Y′)) | ξ] over f, g of zero conditional mean and unit conditional variance; the ROW ENTRY is R(Y, Y′) = ess sup_ξ ρ(Y, Y′ | ξ) (M2's L² form); HYPOTHESIS I AT THE BLOCK SCALE in THE GROUND's form is the weighted row sum Σ_{Y′ ≠ Y} R(Y, Y′) e^{κ|Y − Y′|} < 1 for some κ > 0, every site alike by translation. It is one predicate; the certified strong-coupling row (Theorem 8.7, the decimated measure of the perfect cover) is a different one, and AD's single-link row (one coarse link against its 122 neighbours, 1.163904 ± 0.024706 at the ordered environment on the fitted twelve-term action — the kill-switch) is a third: the single link is not THE GROUND's site variable, and a bundle's correlation with a neighbouring bundle is neither the sum nor the maximum of its links' (the four links of a site are read TOGETHER, a bundle's zero-mean functions including the products across its links).

**35.2 WHAT IS READ, AND WHAT EACH READING CAN SAY.** (a) THE ORDERED ENVIRONMENT ξ₀ (every other bundle the identity). By Lemma C (the fitted density continuous and positive) R(Y, Y′) ≥ ρ(Y, Y′ | ξ₀) entry by entry, so a row sum ≥ 1 at ξ₀ REFUTES the hypothesis on the fitted action and a row sum below one PROVES NOTHING by itself; the reading at ξ₀ is the premise of any environment criterion, never its certificate — §34's correction of YM-2B stands. (b) THE GIBBS-TYPICAL ROW beside it: ρ at environments drawn from the fitted coarse measure, the typical size (0.78–0.82 against 1.16 for the single link, AD §12 (v)); it bounds nothing. (c) THE FIRST-ORDER ROW, EXACT ON THE FITTED COUPLINGS: Lemma E lifted from a link to a bundle — at the free environment, to first order in the couplings, the spin-j components of the two bundles are coupled by the 4 × 4 matrix T^{(j)}_{ab}(Y, Y′) = Σ_{loops ℓ ∋ (Y, a), (Y′, b)} t_{ℓ,j}/d_j² (a loop through link a of Y and link b of Y′ couples their spin-j blocks at t/d_j²; the blocks of different j orthogonal at first order), so ρ₁(Y, Y′) = max_j ‖T^{(j)}(Y, Y′)‖₂ (the largest singular value, the bundle's zero-mean functions including the cross-link products) and the first-order row is Σ_{Y′} ρ₁(Y, Y′) — a finite exact arithmetic on a reading (the couplings) with no quadrature; AD's "≈ 0.64 from plaquettes alone" is its plaquette-only value. (d) THE EXACT ROW AT ξ₀ on the fitted action: the pair law p(u, v | ξ₀) ∝ exp Σ_ℓ t_ℓ χ_ℓ(u, v, 1) over SU(2)⁴ × SU(2)⁴, 24 real dimensions — the single link's six were the quadrature's limit (4 × 10⁻¹³) — so the bundle's exact maximal correlation is read by Monte Carlo on the eight links (a READING) with (c) as its exact companion and the single-link row as its known answer.

**35.3 THE ENGINE (house, no Python instrument runs of record).** ymc.c (AD's sampler, C; the exact Wilson weight) supplies the configurations (24⁴ × 400 and 32⁴ × 150 exist in the record); the score-matching fit of the coarse couplings is one linear solve on the blocked loop averages (a C or awk port of fit.py, the known answer the fit's own table: plaq_½ = 2.19976(24) at n = 1, 0.13082(94) at n = 4; AD's fit.py the foreign anchor); the first-order bundle row (c) is a C program on the loop census through a site (sap.py's polygon counts 6 / 114 / 2868 at lengths 4 / 6 / 8 as the known answer of the census); the exact bundle row at ξ₀ (d) is a C Monte Carlo of the eight-link conditional law under the fitted action, the maximal correlation estimated as the top singular value of the empirical conditional-expectation operator on the character basis to spin 1 per link (a basis truncation, said so), the single-link row's 1.163904 reproduced by the same code with the bundle width one as its KAT. Order of build: the fit port and its KAT; the census and the first-order row; the Monte Carlo row at ξ₀ with the single-link KAT; the Gibbs-typical row; then the n = 8 bundle row (AD read 0.28 ± 0.08 for the single link at a_c = 8a).

**35.4 THE KNOWN ANSWERS.** The fit at n = 1 returns β = 2.2 (KAT of the fitter); the single-link row at ξ₀ on the twelve-term fit 1.163904 ± 0.024706 (the bundle code at width one); the photon's bundle row EXACT by the Schur complement of the blocked Gaussian covariance at n = 2, 4 (the abelian side's known answer of the bundle machinery; the single-link photon row is exactly 3 at n = 1); the U(1) refusal (the averaged row fails wherever the photon is massless, M2's Theorem 4.1) reproduced by the bundle code on the Villain configurations.

**35.5 THE STAKES (written before the engine exists).** S-CP1: the first-order bundle row at a_c = 4a on the twelve-term fit lies in [0.5, 1.3], its side of one UNGUESSED (the plaquette-only first order is 0.64; the six-link loops carried 0.55 of the single link's 1.16, and in the bundle form a loop through two links of one site counts once). S-CP2: the Monte Carlo bundle row at ξ₀ ≥ its first-order value (the first order discards the positive higher-spin couplings' squares) — a direction, not a number. S-CP3: the Gibbs-typical bundle row below the ordered one (Lemma D's direction, read not proved). S-CP4: at a_c = 8a the bundle row at ξ₀ below one (the single link read 0.28 ± 0.08). KILL-SWITCH: a bundle row ≥ 1 at ξ₀ at a_c = 4a is a refutation of Hypothesis I on the fitted action in THE GROUND's own form, and then the crossing scale lies in (4a, 8a] as the single-link reading already says.

**35.6 THE PRICE AND THE STATUS.** The fit port minutes; the census and the first-order row seconds; the Monte Carlo row at ξ₀ an eight-link sampler at ~10⁷ sweeps, minutes; the n = 8 row needs the 32⁴ record (exists). **§35 RESULTS 2 — RESULTS 1's FIRST-ORDER BUNDLE ROWS WITHDRAWN: THE ENGINE COUNTED A LOOP WITH TWO LINKS IN ONE BUNDLE AS TWO COHERENT SINGLE-LINK COUPLINGS; THE CORRECTED LAW, THE SAMPLER'S KNOWN ANSWERS, AND THE READING THAT EXPOSED THE ERROR (2026-11-03 07:44 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, law 18 on its own RESULTS 1 of 07:2x; the sampler `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_mc.c` landed with this note; nothing above edited).** THE ERROR. cp_row.c built the first-order coupling matrix T_{ab} by crediting every loop through the link a of the origin's bundle and the link b of the neighbour's bundle with t/d_j², with EVERY OTHER LINK OF THE LOOP TAKEN AS THE IDENTITY — including the other links of the two bundles themselves. For the single-link form that is right (its conditional law fixes every other link). For the bundle form it is wrong wherever a loop holds two links of one bundle: the plaquette at the origin in the plane (a, ν) holds the links a and ν of the origin's bundle and the link ν of the bundle at e_a, and cp_row entered it TWICE, as (a → ν) and as (ν → ν), each t/4, coherently in one column — a nearest site's plaquette matrix then has operator norm 2 × t/4 = t/2 (the 0.1088 of RESULTS 1's table). THE CORRECT FIRST-ORDER LAW: a loop with links a₁..a_p in one bundle and b₁..b_q in the other couples the normalised matrix elements of the PRODUCT P = V_{a₁}^{±}⋯V_{a_p}^{±} (a degree-p function of the first bundle) to those of the product Q of the b's, at strength t/d_j² (Schur orthogonality; the product of Haar elements is Haar), under the pairing fixed by the orientation; loops with the same (P, Q) add coherently, loops with orthogonal parts add as separate blocks (D(V_a), D(V_{a′}) and D(V_aV_{a′}) are mutually orthogonal under Haar). So the plaquettes at a site (p = 2, q = 1) are ISOLATED entries t/4 between a product of the origin's bundle and a single link of the neighbour's, one per plane, maximal correlation t/4 and not a coherent sum; the longer loops through one link of each bundle (p = q = 1) populate the single-link block as in the single-link form; loops with two links in each bundle the product-to-product block. ON THE PLAQUETTE COUPLINGS ALONE the first-order bundle row at a_c = 4a is twenty sites — the eight nearest (p = 2, q = 1, three entries each) and the twelve at e_μ − e_ν (p = q = 1, the plaquette at base −e_ν in the plane (μ, ν)) — at t/4 = 0.0321 each: 0.642, AD's own estimate ("≈ 0.64 from plaquettes alone"), NOT RESULTS 1's 0.916; the full first order with the six-link loops is owed to a corrected engine that keeps the loops' parts (cp_row2.c, next). WITHDRAWN, DATED: RESULTS 1's bundle rows 15.41 / 17.99 / 1.885 ± 0.041 / 1.869 ± 0.054 / 0.378 ± 0.175, the "one and a half times the single-link row" law, the "S-CP1 refuted upward" verdict (S-CP1's [0.5, 1.3] is live again, UNGUESSED), the by-distance table and the per-site table. WHAT STANDS: the single-link rows (9.908 / 12.300 / 1.256 ± 0.028 / 1.249 ± 0.038 / 0.279 ± 0.114 — every loop a two-point coupling there), the fit's digit-for-digit reproduction, the loop census, the n = 8 couplings, the jackknife correction of run d. THE SAMPLER (cp_mc.c: Metropolis on the pair's links under the fitted action with every other link the identity; the maximal correlation read on a basis of matrix-element functions by a SPLIT-SAMPLE estimate — the function pair chosen on one half of the blocks, its correlation read on the other, so the bias is DOWNWARD; the in-sample top singular value is printed beside it and is biased upward by the noise: the dead instrument reads 0.049 naive against 0.006 ± 0.004 split). KNOWN ANSWERS MET: two links coupled by ONE loop e^{t w(V_aV_b⁻¹)}, whose pair law is a class function of V_aV_b⁻¹ so the maximal correlation is EXACTLY I₂(t)/I₁(t) (a class-function kernel acts on the spin-j matrix elements by a_j/d_j; a₀ = 2I₁/t, a_½ = 4I₂/t): 0.464391 ± 0.002673 at t = 2.2 against 0.464479; 0.033481 ± 0.002185 at t = 0.13082 against 0.032682; the dead instrument 0.0058 ± 0.0036. THE READING THAT EXPOSED THE ERROR: the nearest bundle pair (0, e₀) at a_c = 4a on the single-link basis (52 functions a bundle: 2w, 2x, 2y, 2z and √3·R_ij of each link) reads 0.0230 ± 0.0026 (and 0.0193 ± 0.0027 at −e₀), against RESULTS 1's first order 0.1088 — impossible under RESULTS 1's law, since a bundle's correlation cannot fall below the direct single-link couplings its basis contains; the plaquette coupling of that pair lives on the PRODUCT of two links of the origin's bundle, which the single-link basis sees only through the links' means (⟨w⟩ ≈ 0.13–0.18 per link at the ordered environment, the record's line). The single link (0, 0) against (e₀, 1) reads 0.0520 ± 0.0030 (657 and 221 loops through the bundle and the link pair). NEXT, this lane: cp_row2.c (the first order with the loops' parts) and cp_mc with the spin-½ products of the bundle's link pairs in its basis (96 more functions a bundle); the bundle rows then read anew, S-CP1 to S-CP4 judged on them.

**§35 RESULTS 3 — THE FIRST-ORDER BUNDLE ROW WITH THE LOOPS' PARTS: AT a_c = 4a THE RIGOROUS FIRST-ORDER UPPER BOUND IS 2.635, THE PARTS ESTIMATE 0.905, THE PLAQUETTES ALONE 0.654; THE SAMPLER WITH THE PRODUCT FUNCTIONS READS THE NEAREST PAIR AT 0.034 ± 0.003 AGAINST THE PARTS ESTIMATE's 0.038 (2026-11-03 07:56 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_row2.c` and its record `cp_row2.2026-11-03a.txt` landed with this note; the sampler's class-wise row running).** THE CORRECTED ENGINE (cp_row2.c): for every loop through the origin's bundle and a neighbour's, the loop's P-part (its links in the origin's bundle, in cyclic order with orientations) and Q-part (the neighbour's) are kept; three readings per site pair with the spins as orthogonal blocks — TRIANGLE U(y) = max_j Σ_ℓ |t_{ℓ,j}|/d_j², a rigorous upper bound of the first-order maximal correlation (the norm of a sum is at most the sum of the norms, a loop's coupling has norm |t|/d²); PARTS, the entries of equal (P, Q) summed and distinct keys taken as orthogonal, the operator norm of the sparse matrix — an ESTIMATE (products in different orders are not orthogonal in general); PLAQUETTES ALONE. THE KNOWN ANSWER EXACT: the single-link triangle sum 1.264305 = cp_row.c's aligned single-link row and Lemma E's form (every loop p = q = 1 there), 122 neighbour links. THE DEAD INSTRUMENT zero. THE PLAQUETTE ALONE (0.13082): the nearest site's three isolated entries read 0.032705 as parts (0.098115 as the triangle's sum of three), the row 0.654100 as parts = 20 × t/4 — RESULTS 2's desk number, AD's 0.64 — and 1.177 as the triangle bound. THE FULL FIRST ORDER AT a_c = 4a (the n = 4 FULL couplings): TRIANGLE 2.635183 (rigorous upper bound), PARTS 0.904885 (estimate), PLAQUETTES-ALONE triangle 1.156050; the nearest sites 0.178 / 0.038 / 0.096 with 38–40 distinct keys each; at a_c = 2a 28.175940 / 8.077531 / 8.620020 and at 8a 0.590357 / 0.141532 / 0.051390 (triangle / parts / plaquettes, the n = 8 couplings at their large errors). THE SAMPLER WITH THE PRODUCT FUNCTIONS (cp_mc.c, 148 functions a bundle: the 13 of each link and the 16 products of the spin-½ coordinates of each link pair; the one-loop known answer 0.4609 ± 0.0024 against 0.4645 — the split estimate's downward bias at 10⁵ measurements — and the dead instrument 0.0001 ± 0.0038): the nearest bundle pair at 4a reads 0.0339 ± 0.0031 (RESULTS 2's single-link basis 0.0230: the product functions carry the plaquette coupling, as the corrected law says), the pair at e₀ − e₁ 0.0147 ± 0.0031, at −e₁ 0.0261 ± 0.0025 — each a lower bound in expectation of its pair's maximal correlation and each within the parts estimate (0.038, 0.033, 0.038), the split estimate diluted where the signal is one entry against a 148-dimensional noise; the class-wise row (one representative per S₄-class of sites, 2 × 10⁶ sweeps each, the class size by the coordinate multiset) is running and lands as RESULTS 4. **§33 ADDENDUM, NOTE 17 — (2.36) READ VERBATIM ON PAGE 19: THE BARE INEQUALITY, THE HULL OF THE ENLARGEMENT, NO HYPOTHESIS ON Z_i — NOTE 11's WRITTEN FORM CORRECTED, THE REFUTATION SHARPENED AND MADE METRIC-INDEPENDENT (2026-11-03 08:16 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat, law 18 on its own NOTE 11 after a reviewer's question this lane could not answer from the pages it had read — p. 19 is M3c's `cmp116_19.png`; nothing above edited).** THE PAGE (CMP 116 p. 19, read): "The set Z₀ determining it is a union of connected components, Z₀ = ∪ Z_i, and we denote by Z′_i the smallest localization domain from D_{k+1} containing Z̃_i"; "We extract exp(−δκd_k(Z_i)) from each exponential in (2.35), corresponding to one of these components. **§33 ADDENDUM, NOTE 19 — THE VACUITY THEOREM ABOVE THE BARE KERNEL: THE SPAN LEMMA FOR EVERY COORDINATE, THE INTERVAL CLAUSE ENUMERATED AT L = 11, SHARP AT n = L; THE DESK BOUND n ≤ L − 2 SHARPENED TO n ≤ L − 1 (2026-11-03 08:27 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; `STDLIB/build/aperanton/ladder/Vacuity.lean` and its check `Vacuity.lean.2026-11-03a.txt` landed with this note, with the ladder's run c).** THE THEOREM (Lean 4 v4.35.0, no import, no axiom, no placeholder; omega, simp and decide the proof terms; checked standalone beside the running development gate): a wall-connected ordering of n cubes (`Conn`: every cube after the first shares a wall with an earlier one — any wall-connected family has one, breadth-first from any cube) has, for every coordinate projection moving by at most one across a wall (`adj_a` .. `adj_d` from the wall relation's four cases, by omega), any two values differing by at most n − 1: `span_gen` by induction on the ordering, the four coordinates its instances (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound — the kernel's standard axioms, from the Decidable and List instances). The interval clause: the enlargement of a family spanning the cells lo .. hi is [lo − 1, hi + 2], and a big cube of index i (the cell [Li, Li + L]) meets it with positive measure iff Li < hi + 2 and Li + L > lo − 1 (`meetsL`); `vacuity_at_L11` enumerates, for every extent e ≤ 9 (n ≤ 10 cubes) and every placement lo ∈ 0 .. 10, that no index has both neighbours meeting — the cubes met are at most two and consecutive, so every one contains the shared wall's corner and the coarse hull's linear size is zero (NO AXIOM, by decide); `vacuity_sharp_at_L11`: at n = 11 = L the placement lo = 0 meets three consecutive cubes (NO AXIOM). THE DESK BOUND SHARPENED: NOTES 11 and 17 said n ≤ L − 2; between integer endpoints three consecutive big cubes need the enlargement's length n + 2 ≥ L + 2, i.e. n ≥ L, so the vacuity holds for n ≤ L − 1 — n ≤ 10 at L = 11 (the ladder's own `jproper` had it right; its printed line said L − 2 and is corrected in run c, `ladder_house.2026-11-03c.txt`, LADDER-MET). What the kernel now holds of NOTE 11's refutation and its context: the 3⁴ hull of the one-big-cube domain, the corner cubes' coordinate disjointness, 30 ≥ 45 and 2δ ≥ 3δ false (Hull3.lean), and the vacuity of every domain of at most L − 1 cubes at L = 11 (Vacuity.lean); both files go into the kernel tree and the proof's kernel/ when the development gate's run a ends.

**§35 RESULTS 4 — THE SAMPLED BUNDLE ROW AT THE ORDERED ENVIRONMENT: 0.851 ± 0.035 AT a_c = 4a, BELOW ONE — THE KILL-SWITCH DOES NOT FIRE IN THE GROUND's OWN VARIABLE WHERE THE SINGLE-LINK FORM READ 1.164; THE SAMPLER's KNOWN ANSWERS MET AGAINST THE EXACT QUADRATURE AT FOUR ORBITS (2026-11-03 10:08 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; the records `STDLIB/build/aperanton/coarse_point/cp_mc_row.2026-11-03b.txt` and `cp_mc_kat.2026-11-03a.txt` landed with this note; §35.5's stakes unedited).** THE KNOWN ANSWERS FIRST (cp_mc.c in its single-link mode — one link against one neighbour link, every other link the identity — at 2 × 10⁶ sweeps against AD's EXACT quadrature of the same pair laws, orbit by orbit): the neighbour (e₀, 1) reads 0.052326 ± 0.001389 against the exact 0.05160069; the same-site neighbour (0, 1) 0.051816 ± 0.001259 against 0.05160069 (one orbit under the reflection of axis 0 about the link's midpoint); the collinear (−e₀, 0) 0.000574 ± 0.001333 against 0.00076500; the parallel (−e₁, 0) 0.038284 ± 0.001405 against 0.04072241 (1.7 of its errors below, the lower-bound direction of the split estimate) — MET at all four, beside the one-loop pair's I₂/I₁ and the dead instrument (RESULTS 2). THE ROW (cp_mc_row.sh: one representative per S₄-class of neighbour sites, the class size by the coordinate multiset, 2 × 10⁶ sweeps a class, the product functions in the basis; 58 classes sharing no loop with the origin's bundle INDEPENDENT at the ordered environment and entered as zero exactly, 11 classes sampled): the nearest classes ±e_μ 0.052954 ± 0.001458 and 0.051766 ± 0.000700 (size 4 each), the class e_μ − e_ν 0.036515 ± 0.001293 (size 12), every other class zero within its error (−0.0011 to +0.0007 ± 0.001: e_μ + e_ν, −e_μ − e_ν, the three-nonzero types, the distance-two types) — **THE ROW 0.851116 ± 0.034829**, the sum of class size times class reading, a LOWER BOUND of Hypothesis I's row at the ordered environment IN EXPECTATION (the split estimate's bias downward; the basis the degree-one and degree-two functions of each bundle). WHAT IT SAYS: in THE GROUND's SU(2)⁴-per-site form at a_c = 4a the ordered-environment row lies in [0.85 (sampled), 2.635 (the first-order triangle bound)] with the parts estimate at 0.905 and the plaquettes alone at 0.654 — BELOW ONE on every reading but the triangle bound — where the single-link form's ordered-environment row is 1.163904 by exact quadrature: THE KILL-SWITCH THAT FIRED ON THE SINGLE LINK DOES NOT FIRE IN THE REDUCTION's OWN VARIABLE at the two-step scale. What it does not say: a row below one at one environment proves nothing (R is an essential supremum over environments; §35.2 (a)), and the sampled value is a lower bound whose basis truncation is not quantified upward; whether the exact ordered-environment bundle row exceeds one is decided by a sharper upper bound (the parts' Gram matrices exact) or a richer basis, not by this reading. Per site the bundle pair's value equals the best single-link pair's (0.053 against 0.052 on the nearest site; the plaquette's product coupling t/4 = 0.032 below the six-link loops' single-link coupling), and the per-site parts estimate 0.038 is below the sampled 0.053 — the parts estimate is an estimate, not a bound, as RESULTS 3 said. THE STAKES: S-CP1 ([0.5, 1.3] at 4a) HOLDS for the sampled row and the parts estimate, the side of one BELOW on both; S-CP2 (the sampled row at or above its first order) holds against the plaquettes alone (0.85 ≥ 0.65) and not against the parts estimate (0.85 against 0.905, within 1.6 errors) — the stake named no first-order form and is read on both; S-CP3 (the Gibbs-typical row below the ordered) unread, the typical-environment sampler the next instrument; S-CP4 (below one at 8a) held at first order in RESULTS 3. THE STATE OF THE CROSSING SCALE IN THE BUNDLE FORM: open at 4a (not refuted at the ordered environment), the single-link refutation's bite removed from THE GROUND's form; the certificate of Hypothesis I at 4a needs the essential supremum, for which §31.3's Gibbs-typical row and the straddling lemma's bound are the roads.

**§33 ADDENDUM, NOTE 23 — THE RESOLVENT ROAD: W3 random_3 CERTIFIED UNCONDITIONALLY, THE GELFAND BOX's DEFECT NAMED, THE WHOLE W3 COLUMN AT THE FLOAT's WIDTH (2026-11-03 10:08 EST; the trunk seat recording iii-08's message of 10:0x; 21789e5d7 at 10:00: certspec.c, the W3 record's appendix, READING.md §16).** THE DIAGNOSTIC at K = 4, as this lane asked: the similarity well conditioned (|I − YV| 1.36 × 10⁻⁹, |X|_max 5.43, the couplings into the four isolated blocks 1.9 × 10⁻⁹ to 4.1 × 10⁻⁹); the rest m = 896, in the decoupled Schur coordinates a midpoint of Frobenius norm 1.40 × 10⁻⁵ of which 1.38 × 10⁻⁵ strictly upper (strictly lower 6.1 × 10⁻¹⁸; the largest diagonal 2.29 × 10⁻⁶) — triangular and strongly non-normal; its own radii 2.47 × 10⁻⁸, the coupling into it ε_rest = 4.06 × 10⁻⁸. THE GELFAND ROAD's DEFECT, NAMED: it raised every entry of the 896 × 896 rest by ε_rest, a box of Frobenius radius 896 × 4.06 × 10⁻⁸ = 3.64 × 10⁻⁵ containing matrices of that spectral radius, so no power of it could read lower — over the squarings |rad|/|mid| went 2.6 → 44 → 10⁴ → overflow while the bound stalled at 3.69 × 10⁻⁵; the rise with K was the box growing with the couplings, not the spectrum. THE RESOLVENT ROAD (CERTSPEC_PSEUDO): Feingold–Varga asks only σ_min(B_rr − λ) ≤ ε_rest; with B_rr = U + L + Δ, σ_min ≥ 1/‖(U − λ)⁻¹‖₂ − ‖L‖_F − ‖Δ‖_F, and for every |λ| ≥ R one comparison matrix C_R bounds the triangular inverse entrywise (the diagonal R − |u_ii| rounded down, the off-diagonal −|u_ij| rounded up), ‖(U − λ)⁻¹‖₂ ≤ √(‖C_R⁻¹‖₁‖C_R⁻¹‖_∞) from one back and one forward substitution in upward rounding; R* the least such R by bisection — no grid, no power, no box. THE KAT EXTENDED AND MET: on the known spectrum the rest below √5 and the gap 4.472135954997 against the exact 4.472135955000; at a 5 % entry radius the known values inside (a staked refusal there was WRONG — every member of that box has gap ≥ 2.17 — corrected and dated); at 50 % REFUSED REST-RESOLVENT by name.
